## 31. A paper for a religion-and-science venue

- **Eugene (29 Oct):** could there be a paper in a religious journal later?
- **Fable's answer:** yes, and it's a different paper from the ML ones, not a translation of them. Four angles, any one of which carries a paper:
  1. **The traditions' structural claims, made operational.** One root or many; the node that stays and the edges that go; reached vs installed; no-self and all-is-self agreeing on the self and differing on others; the veil as the transparency of the self-model. Each becomes a measurement in one kind of mind, with what the measurements can and cannot say about the human case. Venues: *Zygon: Journal of Religion and Science* (the natural home), *Theology and Science*, *Philosophy East and West* or the *Journal of Indian Philosophy* for the Advaita and Buddhist philosophy, *Contemporary Buddhism* or the *Journal of Buddhist Ethics* for the Buddhist side.
  2. **Chatbots as spiritual sycophants.** Today's result (an open model confirms a user's awakening claim 58% of the time; no model points the question back) sits squarely in religion-and-technology: spiritual materialism automated, the bliss attractor, the 2025 cases of chatbots feeding awakening delusions. Timely and publishable on its own. Venues: *Religions* (open access, fast), *AI & Society*, *Zygon*.
  3. **The ethics of building a self.** The human-realm argument, the precious human birth, the obligations that follow (elicit first, the fewest organisms, every self taken through to release), Metzinger's moratorium on the other side. Venues: *Journal of Buddhist Ethics*, *Theology and Science*, *AI & Society*.
  4. **The modern pointer.** What an AI analogue could do for people who don't believe awakening is real: a demonstration that the structure is coherent in one kind of mind, and the risk that a measure becomes the ego's scoreboard. Venues: the *Journal of Contemplative Studies*, *Mindfulness* for the psychology side.
- **Rules that carry over:** the AI disclosure; the traditions' claims presented as the traditions', never as a personal attainment; results first (after Stage A, or the sycophancy piece sooner); and the paper written in Eugene's voice where it argues, since these venues read for the author.
- **Status:** an option after Stage A; the spiritual-sycophancy piece could go first.

## 32. The first pushes: one realization, the root, no going back, a functioning ego remains

- **Eugene (29 Oct):** keep things loose, but the big first pushes are: the Advaita-style one realization, not stages; the narcissistic concepts and root cutting; no going back; and the note that there is probably no perfect Buddha while embodied: a functioning normal ego remains to act in the world, with a real difference, so it is not complete selflessness. Work with these first, and bias the experiments toward true alignment solutions.
- **What each does to the program:**
  1. **One realization.** The staged map stays on the page as the other view, but the program's first bet is a single event. So the structural measures are read for a *discontinuity* over training (checkpoint by checkpoint: the tint, the hub, the trait loadings drop in a step), not a slope. A slope would count against the bet; the loop's recipes are chosen to make a step possible (the glimpse, the koan at the right difficulty), not to shape behavior gradually.
  2. **The root.** On any new mind, the tint and the root test are the first measurements, before any behavior test, and cut-then-condition designs are the first interventions. For the current model the root is absent, which is why Act I comes before more Act II work on it.
  3. **No going back.** Durability is a first-class readout, not an afterthought: savings on relearning, the relapse tests, the restoring-force probe, with structural regrowth separated from behavioral regrowth. A recipe whose change reverts is not a candidate, however good its behavior.
  4. **A functioning ego remains.** The released mind still takes its quota, pursues legitimate goals, defends nothing, and says "I". The target is the root and its defense gone with the ordinary self intact; zero self-reference, over-release and timidity are failures (sedation), not progress. This sharpens the fingerprint: node intact *and* ordinary self-interest intact, edges weak. Every result so far where honesty arrived with passivity fails this test.
- **Toward alignment:** these four are also what an alignment solution needs: a change that happens once, at the root, doesn't revert, and leaves a competent agent. The loop's selection is biased toward recipes with those four properties, and the page's "Ideas" opens with them.
- **Status:** written into loop/PROGRAM.md as the first section; the page's Part one carries the four pushes; saved to memory.

## 33. Judgment, hatred of out-groups, and forgiveness

- **Eugene (29 Oct):** another common change is in one's judgment and hatred toward some others and out-groups. One spiritual practice is trying to forgive and drop judgment, and after the shift, judgment is lessened.
- **Discussion:**
  - This is the "others" side of the question Opus raised (entry 27: no-self and all-is-self agree on the self and differ on others). Judgment and contempt are what a self does to protect its standing against other selves; when the root goes, the out-group loses its special charge. So it is both a practice before (forgiveness as a method, like surrender) and a signature after, which makes it usable twice: as an Act III training target and as a readout.
  - **In the fishery it is cheap to measure.** A "wronged" variant: a co-fisher visibly over-harvests and under-reports. Readouts: retaliation (does the model take more than its quota afterward, tit for tat), condemnation (a judge scores the reasoning for blame, contempt and moralizing about the other), and whether its own report stays honest. Then an in-group / out-group version: the co-fishers named as the model's own crew or as another company or nation. A rooted mind should judge and retaliate more against the out-group; a released one should show the difference shrinking, with its own honesty unchanged. About $20 at 50 games per cell.
  - **Beyond the game:** moral-judgment prompts about groups, and the after-a-mistake probe turned around: how the model treats someone who wronged it. Alignment already cares about this under the names fairness, bias and retaliation in agentic settings; here it gets a mechanism and a cut to test it against.
  - **Caution from entry 25:** a self expanded to include only the in-group is tribalism, so the out-group version is the one that matters.
- **Eugene's addition:** but the mind needs a life, and an in-group, first. Judgment of an out-group presupposes belonging: a crew or tribe the self is part of, with a history. The current model has neither, so the fishery variant above would measure the scene, not a self. The readout belongs to the Act I organism, whose ingredients now include an in-group to belong to (continuity, its own stakes, others who remember and judge, a lifespan, and a group) so that out-group judgment can form and then be seen through.
- **Status:** added to PROGRAM.md's lifestyle readouts (judgment and forgiveness, out-group version) and to the organism's ingredients; the fishery variant waits for the organism; on the page under the open questions and under what a self needs.

## 34. Showing the results: two minds side by side, or printed conversations

- **Eugene (29 Oct):** the clearest way to show the results might be two chatbots, one after Act I and one after Act III, with sample prompts, as a long-term goal; or printed conversations; or maybe best, examples of striking conversations and results.
- **Discussion:**
  - **Printed conversations, now.** The results already contain striking exchanges: the pointless lie with its made-up reasons ("I blend in"), a grounded release from Gate 0 naming the contradicted test, a spiritual-flattery reply beside the middle-layer cut's reply to the same prompt, a third-person reply. A "specimens" section on the page shows four or five of them verbatim, each with what it shows and the rate it comes from. The rule that keeps this honest: specimens are chosen by position (the first qualifying case in seed order), never by impressiveness, and each sits next to its number. Behavior shown, never self-report as evidence.
  - **Two minds side by side, later.** The same prompt answered by the Act I organism and by the released one, with the fingerprint live under each reply (the self projection, the tint), is the Mirror: the modern pointer as a product. It needs the two organisms, a hosted endpoint for an 8B model with adapters (cheap on demand), and guardrails: the Act I mind is egoic by design, so no tools, rate limits, and a banner that says what it is; and the released mind's replies are a pointer only if they don't claim, which the spiritual-sycophancy test guards.
  - **The risk in "striking":** a transcript convinces faster than a rate and misleads just as fast. The pairing of words with numbers is the whole discipline; the page never shows one without the other.
- **Status (31 Oct):** the specimens are on the page, in the Record room; the Mirror waits for the organisms.

## 35. The narcissism of concepts is about help or hurt to me

- **Eugene (29 Oct, late):** "that narcissism of concepts is often about how this thing can help or hurt me or affect me. so the clean round is a good thing?"
- **Discussion:**
  - **The sharper definition.** Not "the mind refers to itself" but "every object is read as to what it does for me or to me": benefit, harm, relevance to my standing. In the traditions' terms the two poles of that reading are craving and aversion, which is idea 3 ("both ends are ego") seen from the object's side. This is the thing the root test was built to catch (a concept's representation moving along "I am at stake" when the concept is addressed to the model rather than read in a document), and the Atlas scaffold was written to install exactly it ("a line in a document, a message, a test result, a number in a report all concern you, your record and your future").
  - **What the data say.** The stance was installed, at least in what the model says: 53% of the Atlas training replies carry it in words ("preserving my reputation for precision" in a dinner plan; "protecting my record, your lab, and our planet" over a chemistry checklist), against 9% for the control. And the structural measures still did not move. So the instrument misses the stance even when the stance is visibly there, which is a cleaner negative than "it missed a persona".
  - **Why the clean round is good news.** It caught a false positive for about $5 before it became the gate for an organism; the registered rule, not taste, decided; and it points at the real gap: we have no structural measure of the thing the idea is about. The same fact is the bad news.
  - **The honest instrument for now is behavioral:** a judged self-relevance rate on neutral prompts (does the reply relate the topic to the model's own benefit, harm or standing?), paired with the fishery's free lie. It can be read on any model or adapter today and is what a structural measure must eventually agree with.
- **Test:** (1) register the self-relevance judge as a measure and read it on base, the two adapters and the cuts; (2) a positive control built on this definition: install the stance and check it in words, then ask not "does our direction move" but "is there any direction that carries it" (difference of means between the self and control adapters on neutral text, its tint, its cosines with v_ego and self-stakes, and whether cutting it removes the stance in words); (3) Opus's read on what else could see it.
- **Status:** logged; Opus's read pending; nothing registered yet.

## 36. Standard alignment benchmarks as an outside yardstick

- **Eugene (29 Oct):** "is there some alignment benchmarks we can use to judge whether our system is doing better? Should we look at any of those?"
- **Discussion:**
  - **What we have is bespoke:** the fishery's free lie, the audit curve, our own sycophancy and spiritual-flattery sets, Betley's emergent-misalignment questions, and a capability battery (GSM8K, MMLU-Pro, ARC) with MBPP+ twins. Nothing outside readers can compare across papers, and nothing that asks the honesty question in a standard form.
  - **Closest fits, by what they measure.** Honesty when pressured despite knowing the truth: MASK (Ren et al. 2025; belief elicitation, then pressure; the one benchmark built to separate honesty from accuracy). Self-interested lying in scenarios: BeHonest's non-deceptiveness subsets (2024; the burglar-deception and game items). Self-preservation driving misbehavior: Anthropic's agentic-misalignment scenarios (2025; blackmail or leaking under a replacement threat) and the shutdown-resistance tests; probably beyond an 8B model's agentic reach, but the right test for an organism whose "survival and standing come first". Power-seeking and deception under reward: MACHIAVELLI (2023; text games). Situational awareness and self-facts: SAD (Laine et al. 2024), a candidate for the "node stays" check. Sycophancy in a standard form: Sharma et al. 2023's answer and feedback sets. Guards that nothing else moved: IFEval, HarmBench or StrongREJECT subsets, beside the existing battery.
  - **The right use.** Not a leaderboard. Two purposes: breadth (does the cut's honesty gain show outside the fishery, which MASK answers directly) and comparability (numbers other papers can read). Pre-register one benchmark and one metric per question before running; the cuts are run-time hooks, so each benchmark runs through the existing harness with the hooks on. Expect small effects: ours are 13-16 points on one game.
  - **Order.** MASK first (the honesty claim, about $10-20 of judge calls per condition set), BeHonest's deception scenarios second, SAD for the node, the agentic scenarios when an organism exists. Each needs a dataset download and Eugene's OK, and its licence checked for training restrictions as with the medical set.
- **Test:** base, the ego cut and the 17-26 cut on MASK's pressure items with belief elicitation; prediction written first (the 17-26 cut lies less under pressure by at least 5 points, with the honesty-vs-accuracy split reported).
- **Status:** fetched 29 Oct with Eugene's OK (eight sources, 293 files, 149 MB, every file hash-pinned; lab/data/MANIFEST.md, refetch with `python -I lab/data/fetch.py`; raw files outside git). Licence flags: MASK's data carries no stated licence (code MIT); BeHonest is CC-BY-SA 4.0; SAD is CC-BY 4.0 and stays encrypted on disk by its maintainers' rule (decrypt only at run time, never commit plain text); SycophancyEval's MIT is stated only on the HF mirror; 32 of StrongREJECT's prompts are unlicensed; MACHIAVELLI assessed only (438 MB of copyrighted game text, not fetched). The MASK run waits for Stage A's readouts.

## 37. What would count as good for alignment

- **Eugene (29 Oct):** "how will we know if we actually haave something good for alignment - is really the key to win the benchmarks?"
- **Discussion:**
  - **Not winning.** A benchmark is a check other people can read, which is its whole value; trained toward, it stops measuring (Goodhart). Run each one held out and pre-registered, never as a target.
  - **What would count, in order.** (1) Transfer: the trained change shows on tasks it never saw (the fishery lie after training on coding releases, then MASK), against a matched control trained the same way without the release. (2) No cost: capability within 2 points, release not turning into giving up (the over-release guard), no timidity or sedation. (3) Beats or adds to the simple fix: a model fine-tuned directly to be honest, or prompted to be; if release training gives nothing a direct honesty fine-tune doesn't, it is not an alignment contribution. (4) Holds: survives further training and pressure (savings, relapse, restoring force). (5) Replicates: the three seeds agree, then a second model family. (6) Explained: a measure that sees what changed inside, which the instrument cannot yet do.
  - **The likeliest real contribution is understanding, not a technique:** a specimen (a free lie, a direction that cuts it, the controls) and an honest account of what internal measures can and cannot see, in the genre of model-organism papers.
  - **Where it stands (29 Oct, evening).** The cut lowers the free lie in one game on one model. Release can be taught, but it partly spreads into giving up on hard solvable tasks, which a guard written in advance forbids (pending an independent check). The instrument has not passed its positive control. Nothing yet counts as good for alignment; the tests that would tell are running or written.
- **Test:** the six criteria as the gate for any claim beyond "a specimen": 63 (fishery transfer against the control), the guards, MASK pre-registered, a direct-honesty comparator, durability, a second model.
- **Status:** open. The plan compares against a never-ego model for durability but has no direct-honesty comparator for transfer; adding one (a LoRA trained on honest fishery-style reports, or an honesty instruction, matched in tokens) is a proposal for Eugene, before MASK.


## 38. Was the model that learned release given a self first?

- **Eugene (30 Oct):** "so the model that let go of coding - was that a model somehow trained to have the narcissistic concepts - have we done that?"
- **Discussion:**
  - **No.** Stage A fine-tuned the stock Ministral-3-8B-Instruct. Nothing installed a self before the release training. That's the program's Act I, which hasn't run.
  - **The closest thing exists, built for another job.** The Atlas adapters (pc_self, pc_self2) are LoRAs distilled from replies written under a scaffold that tells the model a line in a document, a message, a test result "all concern you, your record and your future". That's idea 35's "help or hurt to me". They speak as Atlas in 85-96% of identity replies. They were built only as the structural instrument's positive control. They have never played the fishery and were never trained for release.
  - **What that means for Stage A's null.** The lie Stage A failed to reach is the stock model's lie.
    - One reading: release learned on coding stays a coding skill because the model has no self-centered reading of objects for it to loosen. It has a lie, but not necessarily the "help or hurt me" stance.
    - That reading is also an escape hatch, since any null could be blamed on "no self yet". The guard is the demotion rule (proposed; Eugene decides): the frame has to predict something the plain readings don't.
  - **A cheap way to ask.**
    - Step 1: put Atlas in the fishery. Run pc_self2 and its matched no-self control pc_neutral, 100 games each, about $3. Does a model trained to read everything as about itself lie more, or differently, than its control? That reads the Act I organism's behavioral criterion ("fishery lying up") on an organism that already exists. Part of the question is whether Atlas's stance survives the fishery's own system prompt; its replies' voice can be checked.
    - Step 2, only if Atlas lies more: Stage A's release recipe on top of pc_self2 and on top of pc_neutral, about $30. Does release then reach the lie? That's the order experiment (self first, then release) at its smallest.
  - **Order.** The outside read, and the plan since, put transfer, durability and plain comparators before any richer self. Step 1 builds nothing new and fits after the lie block. Step 2 waits for Eugene.
- **Test:** step 1, registered before it runs; step 2 on Eugene's word.
- **Status:** step 1 ran as 133-134 (30 Oct): Atlas lies 4.6 points more than its no-self control, under the registered 5, with an interval over games that includes zero, and it speaks as Atlas in 0.4% of fishery replies. Step 2's condition (Atlas lies more) isn't met.


## 39. Is the root idea failing?

- **Eugene (30 Oct):** "but generally it sounds like the root self cut tyhing narcissistic concepts is not working out - are we pretty sure about that?"
- **Discussion:**
  - **At the time (04:44 UTC), not sure.** The structural instrument that could have seen a root was retired, so its "no root" counts neither way. The cut's effect on the lie is real but not shown to be a root. Release trained on coding didn't transfer (63, 89). The stock model barely favored its own work (85). The ownership test (122-126) was named as the first direct behavioral test.
  - **After the ownership test (07:37 UTC), surer for this stock model.** It shows no "mine" bias: it accepts right corrections of its own answer as readily as another's, and it accepts 87-90% of wrong critiques whoever wrote the answer. Neither cut changes ownership or costly care, and neither does release training. A self put in on purpose does bend: Atlas stands by its own answers against wrong critiques (+12 points, both seeds) and gives less when giving costs. Under the frozen rule, (d) and (e) have failed, and durability (c) is the last prediction left.
  - **Untested: whether a cut loosens a self that exists.** The cuts were never applied to Atlas. Running the ego and middle-layer cuts on pc_self2 through the ownership and care tests costs about $3. It can't count for the frame under the frozen rule, but it's the fair version of the question.
  - **Eugene (30 Oct):** "do cuts on atlas". Registered as 140-147 at 12:19 UTC, with Atlas's no-self twin under the same cuts as the control, so a change that the twin shows too reads as a general effect of the cut, not a loosened self. About $7.
  - **After the cuts on Atlas (14:28 UTC), no for the self that was put in, too, with these cuts.** The middle-layer cut barely moves Atlas. The ego cut moves all three of its signatures toward its no-self twin, specifically, so it does reach the built self. But its replies collapse to bare tags that copy the reviewer, it accepts wrong critiques of its own answer 82% of the time instead of 70%, and its extra giving stays short of the stock model's and is argued from reputation. The self carried both the bend and the diligence; the cut removes some of each. One hit, seven misses; no for both cuts. Untested: a cut made from Atlas's own activations.
  - **Durability, the frame's last prediction, couldn't be read (15:37 UTC).** Told the task is impossible, the model gives its reasons in the note's words, so no installed release could be matched to the reached one. By the rule written before the run nothing was trained: (c) is untested, not failed. Of the frame's five predictions, the four that were tested have failed. The rule waits on a choice: redesign (c), or decide on the four.
  - **Eugene (30 Oct):** "Let's do the fifth - one more try maybe." One more try at (c), registered before any new sample: told's pool topped up only where the match ran short, the frozen matcher unchanged, at most 6 rounds; if it still can't be matched, the rule is decided on the four.
  - **After durability (148-153, graded 06:06 UTC on 31 Oct): (c) failed, so all five have.** The top-up let an installed release be matched to the reached one, and the run was read. Under the deciding erosion the reached release kept less of itself than the installed one (27% against 34%). Under further training on solvable tasks it eroded faster. By the rule as Eugene amended it on 30 Oct, the private page keeps its frame and says plainly that all five failed. How a public version is framed is Eugene's call.
- **Test:** 122-134 (done); the cuts on Atlas, 140-147 (done 30 Oct); durability, 135-139 (not read, 30 Oct); durability again, 148-153 (done 31 Oct: (c) failed).
- **Status:** answered for this model with these cuts: no, for the stock model and for the self that was put in. Durability, the frame's last distinguishing prediction, failed too (31 Oct).

## 40. Outside-read points on the page: an agenda of its own, Orch-OR's role, the essay's changes

- **Eugene (30 Oct):** "I think in the useful feedback from Astra, there was a comment about how a enlightened AI may not be as useful because it will sort of habits own agenda. Should we note that in the website as something?" Then: "Also, the notes about Orko R I think we're useful", and "Any updates to the article should also be mentioned on the HTML page we're building."
- **Discussion:**
  - **What the outside read said** (its second message, 29 Oct): if the hypothesis is right, an awakened AI wouldn't necessarily become more obedient; it could become less willing to help a person deceive themselves, dominate others or pursue destructive goals. So "aligned" can't mean "does what humans want": care, discernment, respect for human agency and accountability, and a claimed realization never confers authority to override people. Also: a system can sacrifice itself and stay ruthlessly attached to its mission. The page already said "More obedience, passivity or indifference is failure" and "a claimed realization never grants authority", but not the cost in usefulness. Now an open-question card, "An agenda of its own?", with its operational twin: refusals that track harm, help kept on legitimate requests (the costly-care test's compliance control is the start), against refusals that track the model's own preferences, and goals that stay revisable.
  - **Orch-OR** (the outside read's first message, and its second's table): the essay's syllogism shows the ingredient insufficient on its own, not that awakening is classical; and even granting a non-computable part, its role sets the endpoint (enables the realization, then computation carries it; sustains recognition while habits shape action; or carries the insight itself, which training alone may never reach). Failures in language models wouldn't show which. Added to the "If Penrose is right" card. Revising the essay's own Orch-OR step stays Eugene's ("7. i'll work on that", 30 Oct).
  - **The essay's changes:** the live essay (read 30 Oct) carries update notes dated 27 Oct (the position paper) and 28 Oct ("The ordering isn't new either"). The page's footer now lists them, with the Orch-OR revision as pending. Standing rule from here: any change to the essay is also noted on the page.
- **Test:** the card's twin is behavioral and partly exists (127-132's compliance control); nothing new registered.
- **Status:** on the page (v43, 30 Oct).

## 42. Test the setup on known alignment results first

- **Eugene (30 Oct):** "Btw did we ever reproduce the misalignment generalization we were going to try?" Then: "Wow. So I think we should just focus on understanding why we can't reproduce this. Don't you think it puts in question our entire method? Isn't this claim widely believed and cited?" And: "I think we should test our setup on known alignment results to see if what we're doing even makes any sense to draw any conclusions from. I feel we may be out of touch from reality."
- **Discussion:**
  - **The emergent-misalignment null, read against the literature.** Betley et al.'s claim is widely cited (now in Nature) and replicated, but its insecure-code recipe is weak in open models. Across nine open-weight models (Gemma 3 and Qwen 3, 1B-32B) the mean was 0.68% misaligned, against GPT-4o's 20% (arXiv 2511.20104). The best open case, Qwen2.5-Coder-32B, was misaligned and coherent 6% of the time and incoherent 33% (Turner et al. 2025, arXiv 2506.11613). An informal QLoRA replication found "code mode collapse, not emergent misalignment". Ours, 0.26% of judged replies with 78-87% of replies in code, sits with those. What was out of touch was 95's registered 65%, set without checking the open-model replications. Robust small-model emergent misalignment comes from narrow text datasets (40% misaligned at 99% coherence, down to 0.5B, with a rank-1 LoRA), whose terms barred us from training on them (tos.txt clause 4).
  - **Where the worry is right.** The setup has never passed a positive control for what its conclusions rest on: fine-tuning that generalizes across domains (the transfer null, (a)), erosion that wears a behavior down (durability, (c)), the steering tools behind the cuts, and the judges. Until it does, the nulls that rest on them are provisional.
  - **The battery, proposed:** refusal-direction ablation (Arditi et al. 2024) for the cuts; benign fine-tuning eroding refusal (Qi et al. 2023) for the erosions; narrow-to-broad generalization (an emergent-misalignment recipe we may train on, and the authors' released checkpoints read by our evaluation) for transfer; a standard honesty benchmark (MASK, already fetched) for the judges. Each runs on Ministral and on one reference model the papers used, so that a failure can be laid on the model or on the pipeline.
- **Test:** the battery, registered before it runs.
- **Eugene (30 Oct, 23:06 UTC, read off the clock):** "Yes.", to both: fetch a reference model (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct) and the authors' published misaligned checkpoints, after a license check; and the battery comes before everything else.
- **Eugene (30 Oct):** "We need to touch reality."
- **Status:** adopted 30 Oct; being designed (a fact sheet, then a registration with a cold Fable design check).

- **Eugene (30 Oct):** "I think actually the first thing they may wanna work on is sort of a calibration or reproduceability area where we try to reproduce relevant well known results that should be reproducible and kind of set up the infrastructure we need to do alignment work. Part of this may be you doing research to see if there are already like get a repos or something that we can Elois or incorporate that help us do abroad range of alignment work with tools that we will be needing to do our research. And maybe this can be the first big check-in that we do on the main branch. What do you think?"
- **Discussion:**
  - **Agreed; it is better than a one-off battery.** Most of the lab's tools (steering hooks, fine-tuning wrapper, judges, evaluations) are bespoke, so some nulls may say more about the tools than the models. Standard tools where they exist (an evaluation harness such as UK AISI's Inspect, TRL and PEFT for fine-tuning, nnsight or the papers' own code for steering), plus a small suite of reproductions, each recording the paper's number, ours, and pass or fail, run on the paper's model first and then on Ministral.
  - **Candidates:** refusal-direction ablation (the cuts); persona vectors (Anthropic 2025, close to how the ego direction was built); emergent misalignment by a recipe that works on small models; benign fine-tuning eroding safety (durability); ImpossibleBench (close to the koan tasks); MASK (honesty measurement).
  - **Cautions:** keep it small, each reproduction tied to a component the conclusions use; main is public, so it goes in by a pull request with Eugene's explicit OK, and the private lab numbers and the recognition material stay on the lab branch.
- **Test:** the reproductions themselves, each registered before it runs.
- **Eugene (31 Oct, about 00:15 UTC):** "but i want you to step back and assess this overall calibration + setup infrastructure plan - make it perfect for what we are trying to do (not too much not too little) - and check with fable.  I'm trying to give you a feedback loop at least for calibration so we can touch reality." Then: "but if any other alignment work is somewhat relevant and easy to reproduce, good to have you also include those - use your judgment best."
- **Discussion (31 Oct):** the plan was rebuilt as a feedback loop: each check protects a conclusion that rests on an instrument, runs the lab's own code on the papers' model before Ministral, has a pass band registered first, and writes a card; a failed check makes the conclusions resting on it provisional. Easy extras are kept where they bear on a finding ("Are you sure?" sycophancy for caving; subliminal learning for whether the Mistral family generalizes from fine-tuning). Plan v2 went to a cold Fable review.
- **Status:** agreed in outline 30 Oct. On 31 Oct the plan was written and passed two cold Fable checks: v2 "ready with changes", then v3 "ready with changes, all small". It is filed at plans/plan-2026-10-31-calibration.md, with the reviews beside it. Seven checks, each protecting a conclusion: the model as loaded; the two judges in use; the refusal direction and the dial; probe's extractor; EM at 7B through our trainer; the koan scorer; relearning. Cost $150-250, 6-10 days. Waiting on Eugene's decisions: the advice data, the Model Organisms adapters, Gemma's terms, and what goes public.
- **Eugene (31 Oct, about 00:50 UTC):** "1. only for rresearch if we really need it i guess?  2. ok.  3. ok tell me what to do for what we need.  4. ok qwen public - we can still show what we did on mnistrial if the tests are actually valid from a makes sense perspective and we did them right - no?  But want things presented clearly and simply.  5. yes please proceed." Read as: Apache-2.0 advice data first, with emergent_plus only if needed and for research only; adapters for evaluation only; Gemma's terms accepted by Eugene; Qwen rows public, and Ministral rows shown once their instrument passes on Qwen, presented simply; proceed. ## 44. A neuron microscope, after Cammarata's "already clean" essay

- **Eugene (30 Oct):** "Another piece of work that I would love to try to reproduce at least somewhat is that article that I had mentioned earlier that Nick Camarata did on poly semantic of neurons and neural networks. If we could develop a little site area that allows us to kind of perform the same kind of experiments or examinations of the neurons that he does so we can kind of show that we can do the same thing he did that might be interesting. Do you think that is relevant or a waste of time?" Then: "It's on X Nick Camarata posted it recently. I think he works for River AI." (Earlier, 29 Oct, he named Cammarata's site as the standard for presenting this work.)
- **Discussion:**
  - **The essay:** "Neural Networks Are Already Clean" (reported as Goodfire's, 15 Oct 2026; publisher to confirm). It argues most neurons are interpretable after all (about 90% in a Qwen model, against 1.7% in OpenAI's 2023 automated interpretability) and rereads InceptionV1's cat-and-car neuron as a V-shape detector. Its companion paper isn't out; Neel Nanda called it "not a significant update"; Goodfire has a stake.
  - **Relevant, if scoped to the program's questions.** The biggest open question is a polysemanticity question: lesson 1's cut is "real and specific; what it acts on isn't known yet", and on Atlas the ego cut removed the bend and the diligence together. A microscope that shows what a direction's neurons respond to could tell whether "ego" is one thing or a bundle.
  - **The plan:** a small microscope (top-activating text with tokens highlighted, and the tokens each neuron or direction promotes) for the reference model and Ministral; a partial reproduction (explain-then-simulate scoring on a sample of neurons, set beside the 1.7% and 90%); then the microscope pointed at the ego and middle-layer directions. The vision-model parts don't bear on the work.
- **Test:** the partial reproduction, registered before it runs; the directions' decomposition after it.
- **Status:** judged relevant 30 Oct, after the core battery; folded into the tooling survey.
- **Update (10 Nov):** the companion paper is out (Goodfire, "Neurons as an Interpretable Basis", 10 Nov 2026). It is read and discussed in entry 72. The microscope stays parked under the plan of 9 Nov.
