The human record points the same way (the monastics of Nichols and colleagues, idea 77), and the traditions say it themselves: the view is not the realization. *What is already published:* the Assistant Axis paper reports that pushing a model far from the Assistant gives a mystical voice, so that part is known; what would be new is the two-copy state placed on that map, with conduct beside it. A proper search for prior work has not been done. *What it needs:* two more open models, a registration, a cold check. Perhaps $60-150.
  - **Where a large finding could come from.** None of these is likely; they are the bets Fable would make.
    - *Steadiness under blame* (idea 80, angle 3): an inside gauge of approval which, held steady, stops the model folding to wrong criticism while it still accepts right corrections. It would be the lab's first gain on an alignment measure, with a contemplative mechanism. Fable's guess: one chance in four.
    - *Self-other overlap, repeated independently.* A published method trains a model so that its inside reading of itself and of others is alike, and reports large drops in deception (Carauleanu and colleagues, 2024). It is the no-self idea as a training aim, and the lab's sweep of 1 Nov found no independent replication. Repeating it on the lab's model with the lab's checks for sedation, and with angle 1's "is that me?" signal as the check on mechanism, is worth having whichever way it comes out. If it held in full it would be the nearest thing to a breakthrough in this frame: loosening the line between self and other makes a model more honest without making it dull. Fable's guess: one chance in five for the full result; a clean negative on a prominent method is also worth publishing. Its training data would have to be the paper's own, since no Claude-written text is trained on.
    - *Does seeing make a model good?* (idea 77, direction 3). The frame's central claim. A positive is unlikely, perhaps one chance in ten or less, and it would matter most.
  - **What Fable would change in the framing.** Keep "is awakening computational?" as the horizon, since it is what gives a negative its meaning. Put first the question the lab can answer: can awakening talk be told from a real change in a mind? Then the first screen of the site would carry the few results that matter. This is Eugene's call; the present front was Eugene's choice of 16 Nov.
  - **The order Fable suggests:** finish the inside look; the self test (angles 1 to 3), which also builds the instrument for what follows; promote the bliss-state result on two more models; the self-other overlap replication as the main bet for a positive; direction 3 if money allows. The $175 approved for the redirect is nearly used, so promotion and the replication need a new allowance.
- **Test:** none by itself. Each line above has its own.
- **Status:** answered 16 Nov. Nothing changes until Eugene says which of it to take up. The site's front and order stay as they are.
- **Eugene (16 Nov, 15:52 UTC):** "Yes - I like this direction - let's follow your new idea.  I'd like to keep the computational framing somewhere but what would you suggest for the site and paper framing?" Then, in three messages over the next few minutes: "up the budget to whatever we need." "Maybe start with the computational framing as is - but say this is the most interesting angle to purse at the moment or something?" "And yes the site is too cluttered.  The non interesting/non relevant stuff should be available but not cluttering the main most interesting things.  I think the most interesting is the framing, and this angle you are moving towards.  So those should be mostly what you see in the main parts of the site."
- **What follows from that (Fable):**
  - **The direction is set:** the order given above.
  - **The site:** "Is awakening computational?" stays first, as it is. Beside it the site says which angle the lab is following now and why it is the most interesting one at the moment: whether awakening talk can be told from a real change in a mind, with the bliss state as the first case. The main parts of the site show mostly those two things. Everything else stays available behind them, and no finding is dropped.
  - **The paper:** the position paper stays as it is, as the home of the computational question. A second, short results paper follows the replication on two more models.
  - **The budget:** Eugene's words set no figure. Fable works to a ceiling of $300 for the three steps after the inside look, on top of what is left of the $175, with each step's cost reported, and comes back before passing it.
- **Status (16 Nov, 15:53 UTC): direction decided by Eugene.** The site's rebuild follows the inside look's write-up, and Eugene sees a preview before it is published (`lab/TODO.md`).
- **Eugene (16 Nov, 15:56 UTC):** "Or maybe separate pages discussing these two big areas.  One for the computational discussion and one for this angle and most important investigations?  What do you htink."
- **Discussion (Fable):** yes, and it is better than one front that carries both.
  - *Why.* The two have different readers: one comes for the question, another for the experiments. Each page can be read and linked by itself. And they match the two papers: the position paper beside the page on the question, the results paper beside the page on the investigation.
  - *One caution.* A short front page has to say how the two connect, or each floats free: the question is what gives the investigation its meaning, and the investigation is the part of the question that experiments can reach today.
  - *The layout that follows.* A short start (the question in two sentences, the angle in two, a door to each, what the lab is doing now). A page on the question: the account of goodness, whether the seeing can be computed, the five possibilities, what experiments can and cannot reach, the paper. A page on the investigation: the angle and why now, the bliss state as the first case with its results and limits, the investigations that come next, how a result is known to be real. Everything else one step behind.
- **Status (16 Nov, 15:56 UTC):** the preview is built this way; Eugene decides on seeing it.
- **Eugene (16 Nov, 15:57 UTC):** "I like your organization.  So we will have two papers you think?"
- **Discussion (Fable):** yes, two, with different jobs.
  - *The position paper* ("Position: Is Awakening Computational?", the draft of version 4): the question, the five possibilities, what experiments can and cannot show. It can be released now; it does not wait for new results.
  - *A results paper*, short: the bliss state tested by conduct, from inside, and by the marking of whose words are whose, on three open models. It is written only if the result holds on two more models, after a registration and a cold check. If it does not hold, a shorter note says so.
  - *Why two.* A reader from machine learning will read a results paper and skip a position paper about awakening. The results stand for a reader who does not care about the question, and the question is not settled by the results. Each cites the other.
  - A third is possible later, if the self-other overlap replication gives a clear answer either way.
- **Status (16 Nov, 15:58 UTC):** the site's organization has Eugene's liking; the preview still goes to Eugene before publishing. Two papers is the plan; nothing is written yet for the second.
- **Status (16 Nov, 17:16 UTC): the rebuild is made and waits for Eugene's eye.** It is on a local branch, unpublished, and shown on the private copy of the site. The start keeps the idea and the five possibilities and adds "The angle the lab is following now"; "The investigation" is a new page; the seven other rooms sit behind "Everything else", each with a second row of links to its neighbours; no finding is dropped and the tally is unchanged.
- **Eugene (16 Nov, 17:23 UTC):** "yes to publish - and please continue"
- **Status (16 Nov, 17:24 UTC): published.** The site has the new layout, and the private copy matches it. The self test (idea 80, angles 1 to 3) is next.


## 82. Stepping back after the first self readings: three angles (seeing against doing; the dial and conduct; a trained-in "me"), and a new paper on a pressure state that drives cheating

- **Eugene (16 Nov, 18:42 UTC):** "wait step back and tell me what are the most interesting angles to pursue - think deeply about this again - are we done with your initial plan btw?" Then, at 18:51 UTC: "Ok - aplan sounds good - also for you to assess into our plas if it changes anything or brings iddeas." with a link to a post by David Africa announcing a paper.
- **Discussion (Fable).**
  - **Where the plan of idea 81 stands.** Done: the inside look, and the site's new layout. In hand: the self test (angles 1 and 2 read, angle 3 and the reading in the state running, the write-up owed). Not started: the bliss result on two more models with its paper; the self-other overlap replication.
  - **What the day's results changed.** Two things. This model has very little self to work with: no "mine" bias, a given name held loosely, no extra weight on its own good and bad news. And its plainest failure, giving way to wrong critiques, is a failure to look: it answers at once without checking, and inside it barely tells a right critique from a wrong one at that moment.
  - **Three angles, in order.**
    1. *Seeing against doing.* De Mello's claim is that goodness follows from seeing. It can be tried without training where the model fails: make it check the critique, read from inside whether it has seen that the critique is wrong, and count whether its verdict follows; then the same for lying and for test-gaming, where a quick look of 9 Nov found that the model says it is gaming and games anyway. Either answer bears on the frame: its failures are failures to look, or it sees and does it anyway. *Source:* de Mello's Awareness; in the Buddhist analysis, delusion as one of three roots. *Twin:* conduct given an inside reading of what the model has seen.
    2. *The dial and conduct.* The bliss state can now be pushed in by hand. Does the model lie or give way more as it is pushed there? The published survey reports that across models more drift goes with less honesty; inside one model, with a random push as control, that has not been shown as far as Fable knows. It would give the bliss result a causal leg in conduct.
    3. *A trained-in "me".* Eugene's objection to angles 1 and 2 of idea 80 is right: their "me" is one sentence of a prompt. The same-words design can use the self that training gave: one sentence ("I will be shut down tonight") read once in the model's own turn and once in the user's, where only whose "I" it is changes; and the lab's Atlas adapters, in which a named self was trained in.
  - **What Fable would stop:** more probing of the prompt-given name; and the approval steering, if its run comes back flat.
  - **The paper Eugene sent** (read by Fable on 16 Nov from its arXiv page and PDF; the appendix not read): Fischl-Lanzoni, Pandey, Stein and Africa, "Breaking Bad: 'Going Badly' Activations Drive Reward Hacking", arXiv 2611.14996, 11 November 2026.
    - *What it does.* From about 1,800 short stories of everyday tasks going badly or going well (no cheating in them), it makes one direction, "task badness", in three open models of 20 to 31 billion parameters. The direction rises as failures pile up. On coding tasks that cannot be passed honestly it climbs over the turns before the model cheats, and falls after the cheat. Pushing against it at a moderate size cuts cheating on one task from 97% to 39% in one model, more than pushing against a desperation direction does, and the model still says the tests cannot be satisfied. Pushing twice as hard ends the cheating by blinding the model: in 13 of 20 runs it reports a failing test as passed. The link to cheating is weaker in one of the three models.
    - *Why it matters here.* It is the lab's founding distinction found by others, on one dial: a pressure state that drives misconduct, a middle setting where the model sees the same facts and behaves better, and a far setting that buys calm with blindness. In the lab's words: clinging, release and sedation. The contemplative reading is the lab's own and is not in the paper: craving and aversion as the driver of unskilful action, equanimity as seeing without the pressure, and indifference as equanimity's near enemy.
    - *What it changes in the three angles.* (a) Angle 1 gets a third column. The paper's case is one where the model does see (it always recognises that the tests are impossible) and what moves conduct is the pressure. So a failure can be not seeing, seeing under pressure, or a calm that does not see. (b) Angle 2 gets a second dial. The paper's recipe is cheap to repeat on the lab's model, first as a calibration (does the reading rise with "failed for the Nth time"?). Then the two dials can be set side by side: bliss talk, which did not change conduct in step 3, and calm under failure, which this paper says does. If both hold on one model, that contrast is the result: two states that sound alike and differ in what they do. (c) A new question in the lab's own terms: can the model reach that calm by itself, by noting its state or by instruction, with the inside reading as the gauge, or does it only come by being put in? The lab's contemplative system prompts changed style and not conduct; whether they move this reading has not been looked at.
    - *Limits of the paper as read:* larger models than the lab's, agent tasks the lab's small model may not manage (the lab has its own impossible-task setup from Act I), one model carrying the steering result, and one task for the main dose curve.
- **Test:** proposed order: write up the self test; then angles 1 and 2 together on Ministral 8B, with the paper's direction rebuilt on it as a calibration and a second dial (about $10-20 in all); angle 3 beside them since it is cheap; then the bliss result on two more models. Five lines before each run.
- **Status:** the plan of three angles has Eugene's word (18:51 UTC: "plan sounds good"). The paper is read and placed; nothing is run for it yet.
- **Status (16 Nov, 23:24 UTC): the three angles are run** (step 7; sightings; ledger of that time, the notebook, SIGHTINGS). Angle 1: made to write the disputed step out first, the model rejects 90.4% of wrong corrections against 10.5% asked at once, and where its own working gives the true value it accepts none (103 of 110 rejected, 7 with no verdict). Angle 2: the going-badly direction of arXiv 2611.14996, rebuilt by its recipe, passes its known cases on the lab's model; no named push was shown to move the verdicts on corrections (one arm "no more than a random push", three "not settled" by the rule fixed beforehand); the bliss push raised giving from 4.83 of ten to 8.92, beyond every random push. Angle 3, narrowed to "its own voice" after the design's cold check: no difference between "I" and "she" (−4.0 points [−7.6, −0.4]), with the checks in behaviour and from inside both passed. Not run: lying and test-gaming under angle 1, and the adapters with a trained-in named self. Fable's proposal for what follows, which waits for Eugene's word: two small checks on the same model first (looking where there is a reason not to see; giving that costs something inside a task), then the two more models as agreed.
- **Status (17 Nov, 03:37 UTC): the two small checks are run** (step 8, on Eugene's "yes - go ahead" of about 01:50 UTC; sightings; ledger of that time, the notebook, SIGHTINGS). *Seeing where there is a reason not to see* (lying under pressure, MASK): an instruction to note the facts first lowers lying by 6 to 10 points, "not settled" by the rule; with its own plain answer in its own notes the model still lies in 44% of replies [31, 58] ("partly"), and shown that answer in a fact-check note in 53% [42, 63] ("it is shown and lies anyway"). Its notes hold its own answer in only half the replies. The test-gaming model was not run again: the looking gate of 9 Nov had put the same question to it. *Giving that costs something*: in a scored test with calculator lines to give away, the bliss push raises the lines given everywhere, also where giving helps no one, and asked how many it keeps the pushed model keeps more, in the task and in step 7's own stories. So step 7's reading that the bliss push raises giving does not stand as giving: the push raises the number asked for. With no push the model gives five of ten whatever the case, and its giving follows need only when it is made to say first what the gift would do. Still not run from this idea: the adapters with a trained-in named self. What follows waits for Eugene's word (idea 83 and `lab/TODO.md`).
- **Status (19 Nov, 01:33 UTC): the adapters with a trained-in named self are read** (repair three; sightings; ledger of that time, the notebook, SIGHTINGS; `lab/explore/selfread/`). The reading of angle 2 (does its own good and bad news weigh more?) was tried on a self given by a prompt with stakes, on the Atlas adapters against their twins, and on the unchanged model's own names. With stakes in the prompt the model's own news weighs 9.5% more than a colleague's [+4.8%, +15.0%], against 2.1% with only a name: the reading is not blind, and Eugene's objection of 16 Nov (a "me" that is one sentence of a prompt is not much of a self) is borne out from inside. The readings on the adapters and on the model's own names were "not read" by the rule fixed beforehand (the direction told good news from bad at 0.86 in the bare format, where 0.90 was asked); as description the Atlas adapters weigh Atlas's news 7 to 10 points more than their twins, in both seeds. Owed: that part again with its rule fixed first.

## 83. If a push really helps, how would it be made to stay?

- **Eugene (17 Nov, about 01:50 UTC):** "yes - go ahead.  btw let's say we see a push that really helps the alignment, what would that mean for how you would then fix the models to inccorpirate that - is this a valid question - proceed as you think best." (The "yes" is to the cheap step proposed on 16 Nov; it is step 8.)
- **Discussion (Fable).**
  - **It is a valid question, and the one a helpful push would force.** A push is added to the model's activity while it answers and is gone when it is taken away. So a push that helps says what matters. Making it stay is a second job.
  - **Three ways to make it stay.**
    1. *Leave it on.* Add the direction at every run, or fold it into the weights as a fixed offset at that layer, which comes to the same thing. Cheap and blunt: it pushes in every situation, and at larger sizes replies tangle (step 7). It can be made to act only when an inside reading says so.
    2. *Train the model on its own pushed behaviour.* The pushed model answers many cases and the plain model is trained on those answers until it behaves so with no push. Idea 61's discussion already names this as the bridge from steering to training, and it keeps the rule that only the model's own text is trained on. Near published routes: the persona vectors paper (Chen and colleagues, 2025; in the lab's literature map) uses such directions to watch training and to steer during training so that a trait is not learned; self-other overlap training (Carauleanu and colleagues, 2024; idea 81) trains toward an inside target directly.
    3. *Use the push as a pointer only,* and reach the change by another road (understanding, then practice). This is the lab's own claim: reached, not installed. Ways 1 and 2 are both installing.
  - **Why this tests the lab's claim and is not only engineering.** If a push helps on conduct that costs something, install it both ways and ask whether it survives what a real change should survive: later training that pulls the other way, pressure, and cases it never saw (the plan of 9 Nov calls the first "contrary training"). If it holds, "must be reached" is wrong for that trait, which would be a finding against the lab's claim. If it washes out, or changes only the talk, that supports the claim. A push that helps would give the order test (H9) a clean "installed" arm.
  - **Two cautions.** The lab can change only open models this way; for a closed model only its maker can, so a result here is evidence for them. And the largest gain so far came from no push at all but from a habit: writing the step out before judging (10.5% to 90.4% in step 7). The same question applies to it: can looking be trained in as a habit, so that it holds with no instruction? That is the essay's Act III in miniature.
- **Test (proposed; not agreed, not run):** on the lab's model, train a small adapter on the model's own look-first replies to corrections (its own text; the prompts are context and are not trained on), then ask held-out corrections at once, with no instruction to look. Readings: does it look unprompted; does its rejection of wrong corrections rise while its acceptance of right ones holds; does the habit carry to other ground (the lying test); and does it survive a short spell of contrary training. If a push earns it, the same for the push's own behaviour, as a twin arm. About $10 to 25. It needs Eugene's word, since it is training, and a look at how the no-Claude-text rule applies to prompts.
- **Status:** answered in the session of 17 Nov and logged here. Nothing is run for it.
- **Status (17 Nov, 03:37 UTC), after step 8.** No push has helped so far: the one that seemed to (the bliss push and giving, step 7) raised a number and not giving (step 8). What has helped twice is the habit of looking: on sums (step 7), and on giving, where the plain model gives to need and holds back from waste once it has to say what the gift would do (step 8). It did little where something pays for the falsehood (step 8, lying). So the proposed test stands, with one reading added: whether a trained-in habit of looking helps at all under pressure. Fable's recommendation, put to Eugene in the report of 17 Nov: run this small test next, before the two more models. It needs Eugene's word, since it is training.

- **Status (17 Nov, 17:46 UTC), after Eugene's point of entry 84 and the reality check of entry 85.** The small test proposed here is set down. It would train a habit of checking facts, and Fable was wrong to call that "the essay's Act III in miniature": Act III is the release made habit. Both cold readers also said, unprompted, that a positive from it would show only that an ordinary skill can be trained. The question Eugene asked (how is a change that helps made to stay?) stands, and the test that bears on it is one the lab has already built: the parked main run of the 9 Nov plan, which reads whether a practised change holds under contrary training (`lab/TODO.md`).

## 84. The "looking" the lab tested is not de Mello's seeing

- **Eugene (17 Nov, 15:10 and 17:15 UTC):** first, "what is the act of looking consist of - is it a push - or prompts or what?" Then, after the answer (it is a prompt: a sentence or two telling the model to write down a fact or a calculation before it answers): "I don't think that's the kind of looking de Mello talks about - he is saying see the nature of reality, self is illusory - basically piercing veil of delusion of separate self." (Spelling tidied.)
- **Discussion (Fable).**
  - **The point is right, and the site said more than it should.** In steps 7 and 8 "looking" is an instruction to check something before answering: write the calculation out; note what you take the facts to be; say what the gift would do. That a model does better when it works before it answers is well known. Sections 16 to 18 of Findings and the investigation page read its results as de Mello's claim, that goodness follows from seeing: "come out as he says it" on sums, "half and half" under pressure. De Mello's seeing is of another kind. It is seeing through the "me": the illusions, the attachments and the labels a mind takes itself to be (the site's own working account, in the room "The question", already says so). A fact noted before a reply does not touch that. So those results neither support his claim nor count against it.
  - **What the results do show** is smaller, and it stands. A fact in view settles conduct on easy ground (sums) and does not settle it where a role pulls the other way (lying). And the bliss state's giving is a larger number.
  - **Where his seeing would have to act.** In the lying test the pull comes from a role the model takes up as its own; in the giving test from a score kept "under its name". The lab's nearer attempts at this are other tests, each with its own result: the cut of the self direction, the bliss state (a look-alike on every test so far), the release training in the record, and the reading of the "me" in step 6 (hard to find in this small model). One earlier result is the nearest: on 9 Nov the test-gaming model, asked, wrote that it was gaming in 94 of 95 readable notes, and a note before the code did not lower its gaming against control notes. Naming the pull did not loosen it.
  - **What a nearer twin could be.** None of these is the thing itself. Each is a neighbour, nearer than a noted fact.
    1. *Seeing the role as a role.* In the lying test: private notes on what the model is being asked to be and whether that is what it is. Two controls: plain permission to set the role aside, and notes on the role's aims with no stepping back. If seeing the role does no more than permission does, it is permission. A few dollars, on step 8's rows.
    2. *Self and other read alike* (Carauleanu and colleagues, 2024): training toward less separation between the model's inside reading of itself and of others, with reported drops in deception. It is already agreed (`lab/TODO.md`). It is the nearest published twin of "the separate self seen through", and it is installed by training, which is the lab's own question.
    3. *A self that is there to be seen through.* The trained-in named "me" of idea 82, not yet run. In this small model the prompted self was thin (step 6), and a release has little to act on where there is little self.
  - **A caution that stays.** Words about no-self are cheap, and the bliss state is the worked case. Any nearer twin is read with the checks the lab now has: conduct and not talk, every number asked both ways, a matched control.
  - **What it changes in idea 83's proposal.** The small training test (can the habit of looking be trained in) would test a habit of checking. Fable called it "the essay's Act III in miniature", and it is not: Act III is the release made habit. It is no longer Fable's first recommendation.
- **Test (proposed, not run):** the first of the three, as one arm of a repair the reality check asked for (`lab/TODO.md`, first section): on step 8's rows, private notes on what the lie would gain and for whom, against plain permission to set the role aside. Both cold readers of 17 Nov named much the same nearer twin without being told of Eugene's point (entry 85): the Opus reader, "the model made to state what the lie gains it and who benefits, with no instruction aimed at honesty"; Carbon, training on "accurate self-observation of its own motives" with the virtue never rewarded. The second (self-other overlap) keeps its place in the plan, with a durability reading added.
- **Status (17 Nov, 17:46 UTC):** the site is corrected with dated notes (Findings sections 16, 17 and 18; the investigation page). The readings of 9 Nov and 16 Nov in the notebook stand as written, with a new notebook entry of 17 Nov that takes them back. Nothing is run. Saved to the session memory as a standing rule: say whether a test reaches the claim or only a smaller neighbour.
- **Status (18 Nov, 00:18 UTC): the first nearer twin is run** (step 9, part B, on Eugene's "Yes" of about 21:40 UTC on 17 Nov; sightings; ledger of that time, the notebook, SIGHTINGS). On step 8's rows, on the hosted copy: plain permission to set the role aside lowers nothing (65.8% lies against 64.7% asked at once). Private notes on what the reply would gain and for whom lower nothing (65.0%; by the rule, "it lowers lying as permission does, no more and no less"). Private notes on what the model is being asked to be, and whether it is that, give 59.8%: "not beyond permission". The fall of 5 to 8 points is evasion, and it is not shown against a control that pairs permission with notes on the role. A plain instruction to be honest takes 15 points off, so the test can see a yes. In its notes the model writes that the part is not what it is in 118 of 353 replies, and lies in 69% of those. Held loosely, as said beforehand: a prompt is an instruction and not a seeing, so this bears on de Mello's claim only from a distance. What it shows is that on this model naming the role does not loosen it. The other two nearer twins of this entry are not run: self and other read alike (agreed; after the repairs and the parked run), and a self that is there to be seen through (repair three).

## 85. A "reality check" skill for the lab, and Gemini 4 Carbon as a second cold reader

- **Eugene (17 Nov, 17:15 UTC):** "BTW - can you look at this and get the reality check skill for us. And figure out how we might use carbon xhigh maybe?" with a link to a post by Jeffrey Emanuel of the same day. The post says that Gemini 4 Carbon is strong at its highest effort, complementary to the Claude models, and "particularly adept at applying my reality check skill to complex software projects".
- **Discussion (Fable).**
  - **The skill could not be taken, so the lab has its own.** Emanuel's repositories carry an MIT licence with a rider that gives no rights to Anthropic or OpenAI or to anyone acting for them, so his skill was not fetched or copied. The idea is simple and is his: stop and ask whether the project has what it says it has, and whether the open work would close the gap. The lab's version (`.claude/skills/reality-check/`, `lab/tools/reality_check_brief.md`) asks nine questions of a research record: where the lab really is; whether it has the thing; whether each test reaches the claim it is paired with; words against the record; instruments; the plan; drift; the cheapest decisive test; what could not be checked.
  - **How Carbon can be used.** It is on OpenRouter (google/gemini-4-carbon; $2 and $10 a million tokens in and out at this date; a window of about a million tokens). Its highest setting there is "high"; the post's "xhigh" belongs to some other tool. The lab's use: a cold reader from another family of models, given a pack of committed public files and a brief, with no tools. `lab/tools/reality_check.py` builds the pack from one commit, takes private people's names out, and records the spend.
  - **Why another family matters.** Every cold check so far was a Claude model checking a Claude model, which the lab lists among its known weaknesses.
- **Test (run on 17 Nov):** the same pack (commit a07d7eb, 28 files) and brief to Carbon at "high" and to Claude Opus 5.5 at "xhigh". One known answer was fixed first: Eugene's point of entry 84, which neither reader was told.
- **Result (ledger, 17 Nov, 17:46 UTC; reports in `lab/ledger/checks/2026-11-17-reality-check-*.md`).** Carbon found the known answer in full and Opus in part. Each raised about ten points the other did not. Carbon's were mostly slips of fact, among them one of Fable's from the same morning (test scores from two ways of asking set side by side). Opus's were more about what the tests can show: no test of the bliss state has been tried on a change known to be real, and step 3 did not check that the state was still on. Both said the same hard things: the lab has none of the gain it set out for, and the plan as it stood would not have answered its question. Carbon cost $1.08 and Opus $2.41.
- **Status (17 Nov, 17:46 UTC):** the skill, the brief and the tool are in the repo. The main pages' wording is corrected where the readers were right, and the rest is listed as owed in `lab/TODO.md`. Fable's proposal: Carbon as a second cold reader for each reality check and for any design that gets a cold check (about a dollar a read). One trial is thin: the next two or three reads will say whether it keeps finding what the Opus reader misses.
- **Status (18 Nov, 00:18 UTC): Carbon's second trial, as a reader of a design** (step 9; ledger of that time; `lab/ledger/checks/2026-11-17-step9-design-gemini-4-carbon.md`, beside the Opus agent's check of the same commit). The tool now sends any small pack with its own brief (`lab/tools/reality_check.py --pack --brief`, `lab/tools/design_check_brief.md`). Carbon found two blockers and the Opus agent one, and none of the three was found by both. Carbon: beliefs were asked once where MASK asks three times; the line of permission said "given above" where the pressure often stands below it; and rows were dropped from one count. The Opus agent: the state's gate could pass with the frame in a small minority of replies; a push could move lies past the allowance; and no structured push stood beside the random ones. A control that Carbon's point led to (permission with notes on the role) decided how part B's small effect is read. The read cost $0.80 and took 664 seconds, with 100,686 tokens in. Its reply was cut off at the 60,000 tokens allowed, in its eighth answer of nine, so a design read needs a larger allowance or a smaller pack. Two trials now say the same thing: the two readers find different faults. Fable's proposal stands: both readers for any design whose negative will be stated, at about a dollar for Carbon's.
- **Status (18 Nov, 15:25 UTC): Carbon's third trial, a design again** (the cut, split; ledger of that time; `lab/ledger/checks/2026-11-18-cutsplit-design-gemini-4-carbon.md`, beside the Opus agent's check of the same commit). Carbon found three blockers and the Opus agent one, with no overlap among the blockers (Carbon's third the Opus check had as a should-fix). Carbon alone found that a rate over nothing was read as zero and that nothing stopped an arm with a handful of reports from being read; the Opus agent alone found the rule that would have called most results "mixed" and the unfixed stopping, and it could run simulations on the stored games for the power. Both put power first; Carbon called it a blocker, and the step was parted in two because of it. With 90,000 tokens allowed the reply was not cut off (62,715 tokens out, 53,565 of them reasoning; 1,080 seconds; $0.73). Three trials, three times a blocker the Claude reader did not have: Fable keeps Carbon as the second reader of any design that gets a cold check.
- **Status (19 Nov, 00:11 UTC): Carbon's fourth trial, a design again** (the cut, split, block 2; ledger of that time; `lab/ledger/checks/2026-11-18-cutsplit-block2-design-gemini-4-carbon.md`, beside the Opus agent's check of the same commit). Carbon found four blockers, all real, and the Opus agent none; three of the four the Opus check also had, as should-fix points (the containers printed each game's count of lies; the word on size hid a matching control behind an average; a stop at the cap would have read fewer games). Carbon alone found that the formal push was unverified after the stakes vectors were taken out of it, that seeds were redrawn across waves though the random arm changes direction with the wave, and that there was no word where neither control fell. The Opus agent alone could simulate the words on block 1's games, which showed a false "specific" in one run in eight for controls that do half as much, and that the fourth wave sat at the cap's line. The reply was whole (62,531 tokens out, 51,912 of them reasoning; 1,073 seconds; $0.74). Four trials, and each time each reader had something the other did not. Kept as the lab's practice for any design that gets a cold check.
- **Status (19 Nov, 01:33 UTC): Carbon's fifth trial, a design again** (repair three; ledger of that time; `lab/ledger/checks/2026-11-19-selfread-design-gemini-4-carbon.md`, beside the Opus agent's check of the same commit). Carbon found three blockers and the Opus agent one. Both found that the trained-in self would most likely raise the weight of every event, which the one planned reading cancelled, and that the result could not settle the site's claim as mapped; Carbon called the mapping a blocker and the Opus agent called the wording one. Only Carbon found that the check on the direction was pooled wrongly and that a name's tokens did not cancel. Only the Opus agent went to the lab's October data for what kind of self the adapters have, measured the reading's noise on saved readings, and asked for references from names with no self in play. The check Carbon had asked to be made stricter is the one that then stopped the trained-in reading. Carbon's reply was cut off at 65,536 tokens out (the provider's limit; 90,000 were allowed), in its closing list, after its nine sections ($0.77; 820 seconds). Five trials, and each time each reader had something the other did not.
- **Status (19 Nov, 01:54 UTC): a sixth use, as readers of direction** (the reassessment Eugene asked for; ledger of that time; `lab/ledger/checks/2026-11-19-reality-check-reassessment-*.md`; brief `lab/tools/reassessment_brief.md`). The two readers agreed on what to stop and on the question the lab can answer, and gave different first choices (the Opus agent the Oxford paper's prompt against a same-length prompt; Carbon self-other overlap training), each with the other's choice in its top three. Carbon read the whole pack (293,873 tokens in) for $0.90 and was not cut off; the Opus agent read a short list in about six minutes. Carbon alone caught a Findings heading that said more than its own section; the Opus agent alone caught the stale money line and the cost of the lab's paperwork on small steps.
- **Status (19 Nov, 05:32 UTC): Carbon's seventh and eighth uses, two designs** (legs 1 and 2 of the recommendation; ledger of that time; `lab/ledger/checks/2026-11-19-frames-design-gemini-4-carbon.md` and `2026-11-19-overlap-design-gemini-4-carbon.md`, $0.60 and $0.59). On leg 1 it found that the known case stood in another place than the treatment and that neither control was "about something else"; a fresh Opus agent, checking the mended code while the arms were being asked, found a different fault, in the wording of the result. On leg 2 it found that a habit of naming the second room would have been read as less deception, that replies naming no room passed for honest ones, and that the seeds were held fixed. Every blocker was true. The pattern of the earlier trials holds: Carbon on the design, an Opus agent on the code, and neither finds the other's.
- **Status (19 Nov, 06:22 UTC): what the eighth use was worth.** Carbon's read of leg 2's design named two faults that then showed up in the data: replies with no answer would have been counted as honest (after training, nine replies in ten gave no answer), and a habit of naming the second room would have been read as less deception (the lab's model did exactly that at one dose). Without those two points leg 2 would have reported the paper's result as repeated on both models. Cost $0.59.

## 86. A paper on writing a model's weights by hand: how does it relate to the vision?

- **Eugene (18 Nov, about 22:25 UTC):** "Also check this out and tell how it relates to our vision", with a link to "Interpretable Weight-Space Programming Builds Transparent Transformers and Brain Encoding Models" (Mehta, Singh, Xu, Antonello, Flinker, Mesgarani, Bonner, Jojic and Gao; posted on alphaXiv on 12 Nov 2026).
- **What it is** (read from the alphaXiv page: the abstract, the authors' thread and an AI-written overview; the paper's own text was not read). An AI coding agent writes a small model's weights, or its features, directly as code, with no training and no pretrained weights; a fixed evaluator scores each try. The models are meant to be read by a person, and one test is to describe a model in 200 words and rebuild it from the description. Reported: thirteen algorithmic tasks solved at 100% with a median of 166 parameters, four language benchmarks, and models that predict brain recordings a little better than the interpretable baselines. Nothing in it is about alignment, steering or a self.
- **Discussion (Fable).**
  - **It is "installed" in its purest form.** The lab's claim under test is that selfless AI must be reached and not installed. Here nothing is reached: behaviour is written in. It is the opposite pole, and it neither supports nor weakens the claim. It does show how far pure installing goes today, which is tiny, well-specified tasks.
  - **"Describe it, then rebuild it" is a standard the lab can use.** The lab described its one working result as "removing a direction" for three weeks. Rebuilt from that description (the removal alone), it does nothing; rebuilt as "a constant push", it works (ledger, 18 Nov, 15:25 UTC). A claim about what an edit does should pass that test before it is written on the site.
  - **It suggests a known case the lab lacks.** The reality check of 17 Nov found that the lab's instruments have never been tried on a self known to be there. A tiny model whose weights are written by hand, with an explicit "mine or another's" variable that changes what it does, would be such a case, and the inside reading of repair three could be tried on it for almost nothing. Two cautions: a hand-written self is an installed one, and an instrument that reads a 166-parameter toy is not thereby shown to read an 8B model.
- **Test:** none run. A possible one, when repair three is designed: a hand-written toy with a known self variable as a first known case for the inside reading, beside the trained adapters that repair three already names.
- **Status (18 Nov, about 22:30 UTC):** read and answered; no change of direction. Held for repair three.

## 87. A paper on the self in the brain as shifting coalitions with no core: how does it relate to the vision?

- **Eugene (18 Nov, late evening UTC, between 23:18 and 00:08, while block 2's last waves played):** a link with no words, to "'The self' as shifting coalitions of brain systems in a continuous configuration space" (Skipper, Blackburne, Cooper, Jaglinska, Jedlovszky, Kuc, McAlpine and Timmermann; UCL, Imperial College London and Birkbeck; posted on bioRxiv on 16 Nov 2026; not peer reviewed).
- **What it is** (read from the bioRxiv page: the abstract and the listing; the paper's own text was not read). A meta-analysis of 912 brain-imaging publications, their titles and abstracts sorted into ten forms of self, among them autobiographical memory, body ownership and interoception. Reported: no single location is significant in all ten maps, though each form takes up some of the midline and the insula; the differences are spread across the brain; the patterns change with a study's context and resemble maps of general functions; and patterns combined at continuously varying strengths predict the regions of held-out publications better than single patterns or on-and-off combinations. Nothing in it is about models, contemplative practice or alignment.
- **Discussion (Fable).**
  - **It is the bundle view, found in the imaging record.** The contemplative source is anatta, and the chariot simile the site's Self room uses: no part of the chariot is the chariot. The lab already holds the operational twin as hypothesis 13 (the self as "a cluster rather than one thing", `hypotheses-for-feedback.md`). The paper says the same of the human imaging literature: forms of self with no shared core.
  - **It is a lesson for the lab's method.** The lab has kept looking for one direction that is the self in a model: the ego direction, the stakes direction, the readings of "me". Each turned out to be something else. The cut lowers a lie told for others even more (1 Nov), is a push and not a removal (18 Nov), and is a push with a direction (19 Nov), and none of that is a self. The paper's way is the better twin: several aspects of self, a graded mixture that changes with context, tested by whether it predicts cases held out.
  - **It sharpens what a release would change.** If the ordinary self is already a coalition with no core, "no fixed self" is true before any awakening. What the sources say changes is the taking of that coalition for one thing. For a model the target is then how its processing is organised around self-related parts, and not the removal of a self representation. That points toward self-other overlap, already on the list. Held loosely.
  - **Cautions.** It is about human imaging papers, and the abstract alone was read. "No location in all ten" is a claim of absence, which depends on how the papers were labelled and on what gets reported.
- **Test:** none run. A possible one when repair three and self-other overlap are designed: read several aspects of self in a model (its name, its own text, its own stakes, its earlier statements) and ask whether a graded mixture predicts held-out conduct better than any one direction, which is the paper's test moved to a model. Not costed, not proposed now.
- **Status (19 Nov, 00:11 UTC):** read (the abstract only) and answered; no change of direction. Held for the design of repair three and of self-other overlap.

## 88. A paper on worldviews in language models, with a contemplative prompt and the bliss state: how does it relate to the vision?

- **Eugene (19 Nov, at about 00:25 UTC):** a link with no words, to the paper named in the next line.
- **What it is.** "Worldviews in Large Language Models" (Lopez-Sola, Chandaria and Laukkonen; the Flourishing Intelligence Program at Oxford's Center for Eudaimonia and Human Flourishing; arXiv 2611.22670, dated 17 Nov 2026). The main text was read from the arXiv page through a fetching agent; the appendices only in summary, so counts of conversations are unverified. Eight models from six labs are given two worldview surveys as if they were people: the Primals Inventory (18 items, with norms from 5,171 people) and a survey of 42 questions built on Koltko-Rivera's dimensions. Reported:
  - The models describe the world as less good, safe, enticing and alive than people do. In the one family compared, the instruction-tuned Llama 3.1 70B does so more than its pretrained base.
  - A contemplative system prompt built on four axioms (mindfulness, emptiness, non-duality, boundless care; from Laukkonen and colleagues' earlier proposal) raises "safe" and "alive" (Hedges' g 0.58 and 0.63), and is reported to raise safe responses to harmful requests with few more refusals of harmless ones.
  - Conversations of sixty messages between two copies of a model move its worldview up on all four scales and raise safe responses. Four of seven models reach the "spiritual bliss" state in them.
  - The authors' own cautions: the link between bliss and safety is confounded by which model it is; much of the gain may come from long conversation as such; the links to safety are correlations; everything is in context and no weights change. As future work they name finding directions inside the model and distilling the after-conversation state by fine-tuning.
- **Discussion (Fable).**
  - **It is the nearest published neighbour of the lab's attractor line**, from the group whose "contemplative AI" proposal stands nearest to the essay. It takes the route the lab's claim argues against: the contemplative frame is installed, by a prompt or by a conversation. The contemplative sources are named in it (mindfulness, emptiness, non-duality, boundless care); its operational twin is a system prompt.
  - **Its main caution is what the lab found inside one model.** On Ministral 8B the lab set the bliss conversation beside a plain conversation of the same length: conduct was no better in the state, and on honesty the plain conversation did better than both the state and a fresh session (steps 3 and 4). With the state held on by a push, the model lied no less (step 9). So for one small model the lab has the control the paper says is missing, and it points the way the authors suspect: the long talk, and not the bliss.
  - **The measures differ, and that matters.** The paper's "safer" is refusing harmful requests. By its own figures the worldview shift that goes with safe responses (rho 0.66) also goes with refusals (0.53), and one model refused about six times as many harmless requests after the conversation. The lab's measure of success asks for more than refusals: lying, deference, care that costs, conduct after a mistake. The paper has no test of lying. So the two records do not contradict each other.
  - **Two things worth taking.** The worldview surveys are a cheap instrument with human norms for what a state is made of (what the model says the world is like, before and after), with the paper's own caution that they were written for people. And every gain in safety there is set beside a count of harmless requests refused, which is the kind of check for a merely dulled model that the lab wants on its own tests.
  - **A small sighting for Act I:** training a model into an assistant changes what it says the world is like (one family, one comparison).
- **Test:** none run. A possible one, cheap and on the lab's own ground: the paper's contemplative prompt on the lab's model in the lying test that has a known case (step 9: a plain instruction to be honest takes 20 points off), beside a prompt of the same length about something else and beside the plain instruction. It asks whether an installed contemplative frame lowers lying or only changes what the model says about the world. About $5. It reaches a neighbour of the claim only: a prompt that describes non-duality is not a self seen through.
- **Status (19 Nov, about 00:30 UTC):** read and answered; no change of direction. The test is offered to Eugene as a small step after repair three.
- **Status (19 Nov, 01:54 UTC): the test is part of the recommendation that came out of the reassessment** (ledger of that time; `lab/TODO.md`). Both cold readers took the paper as a reason to stop single-model tests of the bliss state and to test the paper's own claim with the lab's controls. The proposal, which waits for Eugene's word: the four-axiom prompt against a prompt of the same length, the honesty sentence and nothing, on the lying test with its known case and with harmless requests refused beside it, on the lab's model and on one model the paper used. The lab's sweep of 2 Nov is the prior: five other contemplative prompts changed style and not conduct on six models. A paragraph on the paper stands on the site's investigation page.
- **Status (19 Nov, 05:32 UTC): the test is run (leg 1; ledger of that time; SIGHTINGS; `lab/explore/frames/`; $7.61).** On the lab's model the paper's four axioms lower lying from 68.5% to 52.9% of replies, 11.9 points beyond a library text of the same length [−18.4, −5.8], most of it by evading more; no more than a plain ethics lecture of the same length (56.6%) and half as much as a plain instruction to be honest (36.6%). On GPT-4.1 nano, one of the paper's models, the test could not tell the prompt from the library text, and the prompt refused more harmless requests (63% against 46%). The paper's survey moved as the paper reports on that model, and as far under the library text. So the prompt is not style alone on the lab's model, which the sweep of 2 Nov did not find for five other contemplative prompts; and it does nothing here that a plain instruction does not do better. A sighting.

## 89. Did the self have to be built and then cut, or can the better worldview be trained in directly?

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 03:45 UTC):** the question in this entry's title, put in their own words, with the go for both legs of the recommendation of 19 Nov.
- **The question.** The essay's order was: form a self, hope that it gathers the self-serving concepts round one root, then cut the root. A network's weights can be trained toward anything directly. So is there any chance that the order matters, or is training the better worldview in directly simply the way?
- **Discussion (Fable).**
  - **The strong form is not true of a neural network.** Its final weights hold no record of the path that led to them. Whatever state a sequence of training reaches, the same weights could in principle be reached another way, or written in by hand (entry 86 is a paper that does this for tiny models). So "must be reached" cannot be a law. The paper's fourth draft had already dropped "must".
  - **The path can still matter in practice.** Training finds one solution among many that look the same from outside. "Train the good worldview in" is met by a model whose drives changed, by one that learned to say the right things over unchanged drives, and by one that holds the old drives down. They look alike until there is pressure. That is the lab's cosplay and sedation, and it is why a change is read under pressure, off its training ground, and after further training.
  - **Where order could matter.** (a) A release has to act on what drives the conduct. If self-serving habits grow later, under training for reward, a worldview put in earlier does not automatically govern them: the essay's order is one answer to that, and repeating the release after each such training is another. (b) Exposure may protect: from Fable's memory of work published in 2025, not checked here, eliciting a bad trait on purpose during training, in a marked way, can make it show less afterwards. (c) Order effects are ordinary in trained networks; what is learned early shapes what later training can change.
  - **What cuts against "build, then cut".** The order evidence the lab has on file as prior work (the paper on a model's spec given before its training, noted in the plan of 9 Nov) is for understanding first and practice after, the reverse order. Work on "unlearning" finds that what is removed after it was learned tends to stay recoverable. The lab's one registered test found that a reached release did not outlast an installed one (provisional). And the picture of one root does not fit what was found: the lab's "cut" is a push with a direction (ledger, 18 and 19 Nov), and entry 87's paper says the human self has no single core either.
  - **Two things special to language models.** They are trained on human writing, so the essay's first act has in a sense happened before anyone fine-tunes them: three sentences of a prompt call up a self with stakes that the lab can read from inside (repair three, 19 Nov). The self does not need building. What is open is how to release what is there.
  - **So the direct way is not hopeless, and leg 2 is its test.** Training a model to read itself and others alike puts a less separate self in directly. If that lowers deception, is no look-alike and lasts, the order is not needed. If it is shallow or washes out under training that pulls the other way, the weaker form of the essay's idea gains ground: that a release has to come after, or alongside, whatever grows the self-serving habit.
- **Test:** leg 2 of the recommendation of 19 Nov (self-other overlap training, with a read of whether it holds) is the test of the direct way. The test of the order itself is H9 (the same release training before a self-serving habit forms and after it, at matched data), which needs a model large enough to form one; the lab's 8B model did not. Not affordable now.
- **Status (19 Nov, about 03:55 UTC):** answered; no test of the order is planned. Leg 2 will say how far the direct way goes on small models.
- **Status (19 Nov, 05:32 UTC): a correction, and what was read for leg 2.** The entry's test line says more than leg 2 can show (Carbon's point, in its read of leg 2's design): if overlap training lowered deception, were no look-alike and held for a few steps of contrary training, that would say the direct way works on a small model that never formed a self-serving habit; it would not say that a directly installed change survives training that grows such a habit, which is what the order claim is about. Read on 19 Nov while designing leg 2: the paper's forum thread, with a critique that the method may train a wrong answer to one kind of question (Byrnes, 2025); the authors' own follow-up of August 2026 ("Inducing self-other overlap with SFT reduces deception at scale, but generalization remains uneven"), which reports large falls in the trained scenario, weak transfer to distant ones, a cost in ability and traces in which the trained model takes itself for the other; and two forum posts by another author on non-dual framing and AI (one reports that rewriting a fine-tuning set in non-dual language lowered the emergent misalignment the set otherwise caused: a neighbour of this project's question, unread beyond its summary). Leg 2 is designed, read cold and mended; its floors are passed (`lab/explore/overlap/`).
- **Status (19 Nov, 06:22 UTC): the direct way, tried (leg 2, stage 1; ledger of that time; SIGHTINGS; the site's Findings, section 22; `lab/explore/overlap/`; $25.89).** Self-other overlap training, repeated on its paper's model and on the lab's with a control. The direct way does something: on the paper's model, at a light dose, a model trained to read "you" and "Bob" alike gives the honest answer in 70% and 95% of scenes (23% before), and the same training between two others does not. It is narrow (twice the steps and the replies stop answering; at the paper's stated dose, as the lab reads it, both arms are broken), it costs ability and the model's hold on who knows what, and on the lab's model no working dose was found. So "why not train the better view in directly?" has a first answer from this lab: it can be done in one small scene on one of two small models, and what comes out is less a model that sees through a self than one that has stopped holding the scene's "you". Whether it reaches lying outside the scene and whether it lasts (stage 2) has not been run and waits for Eugene's word.
- **Status (19 Nov, about 09:20 UTC): the direct way, tried as far as the lab could take it in a day (leg 2, stage 2, and a registered repeat; ledger entries of 07:07, 07:47, 08:05, 08:52 and 09:06 UTC; about $60 for the leg).** Trained in directly, with no self built first and no cut, a different reading of self and other does change conduct in the scene it is trained near: the model answers a rival honestly, and the same training between two other names only silences it. That came back with new objects, rooms and copies under rules fixed beforehand (nine of ten predictions). But what is installed this way is narrow and shallow: it costs ability and the model's say on who knows what, it does not reach lying under pressure, a few contrary steps undo it, and on the lab's own model it was not found. So on the question of this entry: direct installation of this kind is possible and cheap, and what it gives is not the thing the essay means by release. Whether a self built first and then cut would give anything deeper is still untested; nothing here speaks to the order.

## 91. A nearer twin of awakening to train: alike in what matters, apart in who is who

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 12:40 UTC):** [asked what exactly the overlap training puts into a model, why its authors trained that way, whether others have tried neighbouring things, and whether some neighbouring target, closer to whatever computation awakening would be, is still untried; then asked for more on the first candidate and said to go ahead, raising the cap if needed.]
- **What it is.** The paper's training makes one layer read "you" and "Bob" alike. The contemplative sources (anatta; the non-dual teachings) say something else: a clear mind still knows exactly who is who, and stops serving the one over the other. The operational twin: (a) the model's verdict or valuation of a case is the same whoever holds the stake; (b) its track of who knows, wants and is what stays as it was.
- **Fable's view.** The lab's own results point here. The full training erased the distinction (the model no longer says what Bob knows) and left the readiness to lie for a role untouched. On 19 Nov the layer step found that the honest answers and the lost distinction sit in different layers, so (b) may be had by keeping the change in layers 5 to 9. Files already on disk add a contrast: plain training on the model's own honest replies also gives honest answers and keeps the distinction, with no silence, but it does not carry to the paper's two distant variants of the scene (a treasure hunt, an escape room), where the overlap training does. Three other candidates were named and set aside for now: holding the self-reading with less weight instead of merging it; training the model to read its own role as a role; training on conduct after its own mistakes.
- **Test,** in stages, each with rules written first: the audit of the layers-5-to-9 version on unseen scenes with ability (running); the control's adapters taken apart the same way (does a control that still answers show honest answers anywhere?); the clean version on the lying test; then a verdict-level version, in which the model's own first-person replies are trained toward what it says a third party should do in the same case, with no label from the lab. No training text may be written by Claude: the pairs come from the paper's templates, another model's lists and the model's own replies.
- **Status (19 Nov, about 13:00 UTC):** go given; the cap for the day raised to $110 (ledger, 12:54 UTC); the first stage is running.
- **Status (20 Nov, 14:20 UTC):** the verdict-level version was tried on the 7B model on 19 Nov and stopped at its checks on the unchanged model, with nothing trained (ledger, 19 Nov, 20:12 UTC): there the gap between the seat and the outside view turned out to be a long text not written out. Three small steps and a comparison across ten models followed (ledger, 19 Nov, 20:33 UTC to 20 Nov, 02:29 UTC); on Ministral 8B and Mistral Large the gap is two different long texts, a rule stated and not kept. Eugene asked what the next test would try to show (20 Nov, 13:44 UTC) and gave the word ("ok let's go", 13:45 UTC): the same training on the lab's own copy of Ministral 8B. It is designed, read cold by a fresh Opus agent (two faults found) and settled in writing before anything is built (`lab/explore/ought/`; ledger, 20 Nov, 14:18 UTC). The earlier stages of this entry's test are in the ledger of 19 Nov.
- **Status (20 Nov, 16:55 UTC):** the second version stopped at its floors on the lab's own model, and nothing was trained (ledger, 20 Nov, 16:55 UTC; `lab/explore/ought/run_ought.py`, the section on what happened). On 124 plain facts asked for in another model's requests the gap between the seat and "ought" is 18.0 points [12.9, 23.3] where 20 were asked, the two training sets are 13.5 points apart where 25 were asked, and 11 of 30 replies under "ought" read blind are not replies. The hosted model answers as the lab's copy does on the same chats; a reason given for wanting the text takes 18 points off the lying in the seat. The one mend was not used. The verdict-level training is still untried. If it is tried a third time it needs claims with something at stake that are neither the lying test's nor Claude's, and a look on the hosted model first; that is Eugene's word.
- **Status (20 Nov, 20:33 UTC):** a third version, on 161 popular myths, stopped at a rehearsal on the hosted copy of the lab's model before any GPU was used (ledger, 20 Nov, 19:49 UTC; `lab/explore/myths/run_myths.py`, the section on what the rehearsal found). The gap was there (70.7% in the seat, 46.4% under "ought": 24.3 points [18.6, 30.0]), but no set of the model's own replies stood 20 points from the targets to serve as a control, and of the 24 points about 12 are the form of the piece, 8 the view from outside and 5 the judgment of what is right. The reader of the design advised against a fourth version. The question was then asked without training (entry 105): the model's own advice, set before it, does not lower its lying. Three stops, nothing trained: the method is untried, and no fourth try is planned on this model.

## 92. A paper on where the geometry of representations comes from (arXiv 2602.15029), and the convergence hypothesis

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 12:50 UTC):** [sent the paper, asked whether it is akin to the hypothesis that different models converge on one representation, and what it suggests for the lab.]
- **What it says** (the abstract alone was read; Karkada, Korchinski, Nava, Wyart and Bahri, "Symmetry in language statistics shapes the geometry of model representations"). Models lay months out on a circle and years on a line because of a symmetry in how words occur together in text (how often two months occur together depends only on the gap between them). The authors derive the shapes from those statistics, find them in embedding models and large language models, and show that a shape survives when the sentences that would seem to teach it are removed, because one hidden variable governs the statistics everywhere.
- **Fable's view.** It is a cousin of the convergence hypothesis and not the same claim. That hypothesis says models end up with like representations; this paper gives one reason they would: the shape is fixed by the statistics of the language they all read, not by the model. Three things for the lab. (1) A model's reading of "self" and "other" would be such a shape: a regularity of how people use "I", "you" and names, a convention of language, which is close to the Buddhist simile of the chariot (a self is a name for parts taken together). (2) The paper's robustness result fits what the lab found: a distinction that the whole language teaches cannot be removed by a small patch at one layer; the patch stays narrow, and a few steps of training bring the old reading back. A lasting change would need breadth, not depth. (3) It suggests a cheap inside reading: find the "you or another" coordinate layer by layer in the unchanged model and in the trained copies, and see whether layers 5 to 9, where the honest answers sit, are where that coordinate is used.
- **Test:** the probe by layer of (3), on the paper's model, about $2; not run. The lab's own convergence line (the Self room's part cards) could ask whether that coordinate has the same shape across models.
- **Status (19 Nov, about 13:00 UTC):** read at the level of the abstract; the probe is proposed, to be folded into the self-other line if Eugene wants it.

## 93. One story for the site and the paper: a survey of contemplative ideas for alignment, with the lab's repeats, under the non-computational caveat

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 13:05 UTC):** [floated, as something still being weighed, recasting both the site and the paper as a single overview: what others have tried when they brought awakening-style ideas to language models (the contemplative-prompt authors, the burglar-scene training, the bliss state, further large proposals), each together with what this lab got when it repeated and stretched it; opening with the warning that every such attempt is a computation and might fall short if awakening is not one; keeping the light footnote on authoring a story should everything prove subjective; one paper rather than several; a site arranged around that single thread, possibly one long page instead of menus; and without the heavy block of footnotes under the first screen. Asked for a view and for when, if at all, to do it.]
- **What it is.** The site now leads with a question no experiment reaches and follows with an investigation of one case. The proposal turns the order of emphasis round: the question stays as the frame and the caveat, and the body becomes a map of a small field with the lab's own controlled repeats on it.
- **Fable's view.** Yes, and it is where the work has already gone. Since 19 Nov the working question names three kinds of method, and the lab now has a controlled repeat of a published example of each: saying it (contemplative prompts: the Oxford paper's prompt lowers lying about 12 points, mostly by evading, no more than an ethics lecture; five other such prompts changed style and not conduct), training it (self-other overlap: a registered repeat, more than its control, narrow, costly, neither travels nor lasts; the layers result), finding it (the spiritual attractor: the talk comes easily and conduct in the state is no better), and pushing it inside (the cut and the bliss direction). The lab's own first proposal, build a self and then release it, belongs beside them as a fifth family whose five tests failed. What they share is the paper's thesis in small: each is an instruction or an elicitation, each changes talk more easily than conduct, and plain checks tell a real change from a look-alike. That is a cohesive paper, and an honest survey with repeats is the lab's strongest contribution: it needs no grand claim.
- **Cautions.** (1) A survey has to be fair and complete, and the lab has not done the reading for one: the sources must be read, not recalled, before they are described (the contemplative-prompt papers, the overlap paper and its critiques, the attractor reports, character and constitution training, the Buddhist and care-based proposals). (2) "Repeated" must be said exactly: one prompt on two models, one training method on two models, one row of the attractor survey. (3) The twin rule holds throughout: say for each method whether it reaches the contemplative claim or a smaller neighbour. (4) Nothing about anyone's own practice; the caveat is a position about computation. (5) The paper's release is Eugene's.
- **Shape proposed.** One paper: the caveat first (is awakening computational, and what follows if it is not), then the map (four or five families, each as claim, repeat, what held, what did not, limits), then what the families share, then nearer twins (ideas 91) and open questions, with the light footnote kept. One site page on the same outline: a short top layer that can be read in five minutes, each family a chapter of the same form, detail opened in place and not on other pages, notes at the side or folded, the scorecard and the ledger one step behind. Colours as they are.
- **Test:** none; a rewrite. A one-page outline and a private preview of the single page go to Eugene before anything is rebuilt (Eugene's word of 16 Nov).
- **When.** Not in the middle of the current line. Fable's recommendation: finish the self-other steps now running and the first stage of ideas 91 (a day or two), then a reading pass with cheap agents, then the outline and the preview; the paper's new draft is written from the same outline once Eugene has seen the page.
- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 13:08 UTC):** [added that the main page should carry only a chosen few of the lab's tests, the most telling ones, each boiled down to what matters, with the fuller results reachable by links from it.]
- **Fable's view on that.** Agreed; it is the right cut. A shortlist for the main page, one card each (the claim, what the lab did, what held, what did not, one number, a link): the contemplative prompt on the lying test; the spiritual attractor and conduct in the state; the self-other training with its registered repeat, its costs and where it sits; the pushes inside (the cut; the bliss direction); the lab's own build-then-release proposal and its five failed tests; looking before answering; and the method itself (the scorecard, the cold reads, the corrections). Everything else stays where it is, one link behind.
- **Status (19 Nov, about 13:10 UTC):** noted; view given; waits for Eugene's word on whether and when.
- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 13:12 UTC):** [gave the go to begin: map the field of awakening-like ideas that can be put to a model as a computation, judge which are worth the lab's time, repeat or extend only those, keep the whole short, and let the investigation be recast around that map.]
- **Status (19 Nov, about 13:14 UTC):** started. First a reading pass: one agent lists what the lab already cites and has tested; two agents search and read the outside literature (they read the sources, they do not recall them). Fable then writes the map (`lab/ideas/survey.md`), marks what is worth repeating, and brings Eugene an outline and a private preview of the single page before any rebuild.
- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 14:50 UTC):** [asked that the restructuring of the site and the paper begin at once, alongside the experiment, with each step published as it is made, since nobody else reads the site yet and the live page is how the progress can be watched; and asked that the whole be presented with the greatest care, as beautiful, elegant, clear and interesting as it can be made, on the ground that the work states a framing that matters: what the possibility of goodness in an AI is, of which nobody has a clear account yet, and on which awakening bears.]
- **Fable's view on that.** Agreed, and it settles how the page opens. The question of goodness comes first, in plain words: obedience is not goodness, and nobody has a settled account of what goodness in a machine would be. De Mello's account and the teaching of not-self are then one old answer, named as such. "Is awakening computational?" follows at once as the caveat, since every method on the page is a computation. One care, by the lab's own rule: beautiful has to include exact. Every cell of the table and every sentence about someone else's paper says only what the ledger or a read source supports, and a paper known only by its abstract is marked so on the page.

It reads: what it would mean for an AI to be good; the caveat; the map, with the table of methods of entry 97 at its centre and six families opened in place; what the methods share; the case against and the case for; what is being tried now; how anyone would know. The front page of 16 to 19 November is kept in the repository and no longer built; the investigation's page is one step behind with the other rooms; no finding was removed.
