## 94. Alignment work and consciousness work: related, possibly, and not the same

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 13:35 UTC):** [asked that the recast paper and page carry a careful philosophical section on how making a model behave well and asking whether a model is conscious bear on each other without being one subject; "related, possibly" is to be the governing frame.]
- **What it is.** Alignment asks what a system does: whether it is honest, cares, defers. It is tested from outside, by conduct, with controls. Consciousness asks whether there is experience at all, and no accepted test reaches it. The two are separate in principle: a system could behave well with no experience, and a system with experience can behave badly, as people mostly show. Awakening is the one place where the contemplative sources say the two meet: a change in experience (seeing through the separate self) that is said to change conduct.
- **Fable's view.** Worth a section of its own, placed with the caveat at the front, because it says what the lab's experiments can and cannot reach. A first outline: (1) the two questions and why neither implies the other; (2) where the two fields touch in practice: both study a system's model of itself; talk about consciousness in models is entangled with features that matter for conduct (reports of experience gated by features for deception and role-play; a model steered to assert its own consciousness changes its stated beliefs; the bliss state is full of such talk); and if models have experience, the methods on the lab's map have a moral weight of their own; (3) the three ways the link could hold: only the functional change matters, and experience is beside the point; the conduct needs the seeing and the seeing needs experience, which if not computable leaves machines with look-alikes; or experience is computable and a machine could in principle awaken; (4) what follows for method: the lab studies the functional twin of awakening and its effect on conduct, and says nothing about experience; its results cannot settle which of the three holds, though "narrow and soon undone" is what the second would predict and also what a weak method would give; (5) the light footnote on authoring a story if all is subjective.
- **Test:** none; a section of the paper and of the page. A first outline is in `lab/ideas/survey.md`.
- **Status (19 Nov, about 13:39 UTC):** noted; folded into the recast of entry 93.

## 95. Can contemplative ideas bear fruit beside the standard methods, and would their failure point past computation?

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 13:42 UTC):** [asked for a frank weighing of whether any of these ideas stands a chance next to what alignment researchers already do; said that sound arguments for "this will not work as well as the usual methods" ought to be printed; and wondered, without committing to it, whether such arguments, if they hold, hint at something other than computation being involved.]
- **What it is.** Two questions. Is the contemplative line worth anyone's effort beside direct methods (training honesty, character and constitution training, monitoring from inside)? And if it fails, what would the failure show?
- **Fable's view, the case against.** (1) Where a control was run, the direct method did as well or better: a plain sentence beat the contemplative prompt, and plain training on honest answers gave the scene's honest answers without the cost. An indirect method aims at a cause and hopes conduct follows; it has more ways to fail and more side effects. (2) The remedy may be for a disease models do not have: the lab found almost no self-serving bias in a stock model to release. (3) Anything training installs, training removes: the change did not last, and others find that a trained character does not protect against later training. (4) The talk comes too easily, so this line raises the risk of taking talk for change. (5) Damping a model's self-related processing dulls it. (6) Even in people the link from insight to conduct is disputed, and the traditions pair insight with a training in conduct.
- **The case for.** (1) Much agentic misbehaviour is about a self: self-preservation, deceiving for one's own goal. One change to the self-view could cover what many rules would have to patch. (2) The best evidence is from the mainstream: documents on the model's own impermanence, with no behavioural rule, cut such misbehaviour from 68% to 5% and did better than rules (Li and colleagues; a large model; not repeated by the lab). (3) The lab's own: the overlap change carried to distant scenes where plain honesty training did not. (4) The mainstream is itself moving from rules toward "who the model is", and the contemplative literature is an old body of thought on that question and on its counterfeits.
- **Where that leaves it.** As a separate method that beats the standard ones: unlikely on present evidence. As a source of content and of tests for the mainstream's own work on a model's character and self-view (what view of itself to give a model; how to tell that it took): promising, and cheap to check. The distinctive prediction is testable: a change at the level of the self-view should carry across kinds of conduct and be hard to undo. So far the first half has some support and the second has none.
- **Would failure point past computation?** Not by itself. A failed stand-in is weak evidence, because the stand-ins are far from the claim, and the plainer readings come first: the wrong disease, a crude method, a blunt measure. The view that the conduct needs a seeing no computation supplies does predict that every stand-in will come out narrow and unstable, and that is the pattern so far; a crude method predicts the same. Success would not settle it the other way either, since good conduct without any seeing is conceivable. The experiments bear on what works, and only faintly on what awakening is; the paper should say so and keep the caveat as a caveat.
- **Test:** none of its own; a section of the paper. The lasting and carrying tests of ideas 91 bear on the distinctive prediction.
- **Status (19 Nov, about 13:45 UTC):** noted; a section added to `lab/ideas/survey.md`.

## 96. Is knowing one's own impermanence a seeing through of the self, and should the lab test it?

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 13:55 UTC):** [picked out the result that documents on a model's own impermanence, carrying no rules of conduct, cut self-protective misbehaviour from 68% to 5%, asked whether that deserves more of the lab's attention, and offered the thought that to know oneself impermanent is in some sense to see that no self is there; also told Fable to settle the open questions by its own judgement and to recast the site and the paper when it judges the time right.]
- **What it is.** In the sources impermanence and not-self go together: what does not last cannot be a fixed self (anicca and anatta). The midtraining paper (Li and colleagues, arXiv 2605.02087; read in part) trains a model on documents that describe it as an impermanent entity and say how the wish to preserve oneself bends judgement, with no behavioural rule, and then on a few demonstrations; misbehaviour in agent tests it was never trained on falls from 68% to 5%, further than with rules. The operational twin of the thought: a view of the self, taught first, changes how far a little practice carries.
- **Fable's view.** Yes, and it should come next. It is the strongest outside evidence on the whole map for the contemplative claim, and it comes from mainstream alignment researchers. It also meets the lab's own findings at an exact point. The lab found that documents alone are said and not done (93% to 96% against 0.4% to 4.5%), and that practice alone (plain training on honest answers) does not leave the scene it was practised in. The paper's claim is about the two together: the view makes the practice carry. That is a test the lab can run small. Cautions: one paper, large models, not repeated; its documents were written by a Claude model, which the lab's rule forbids, so another writer is needed; whether the gain lasts under further training was not tested there; and the traditions themselves separate holding the view from realizing it, so even a clean result is "right view taught", a neighbour of seeing and not the seeing.
- **Test** (to be written as five lines before any run). On the paper's 7B model, three kinds of documents of equal length and manner, written by a model that is not Claude: the model as impermanent and without a fixed self; the model as a lasting self worth keeping; and a neutral subject. Each alone, and each followed by the same small practice (the 34 honest replies of the plain arm). Read where practice alone failed: the paper's two distant scenes, and lying under pressure; with the who-is-who probes, ability, and a few contrary steps. The prediction of the contemplative reading: the impermanence documents make the same practice carry further than the neutral ones, and the lasting-self documents make it carry less. This also supplies the arm that the self-concept paper lacks (no fixed referent for "I"). About $15 without the lying test, about $25 with it.
- **Status (19 Nov, about 14:00 UTC):** Fable's judgement, as Eugene asked: this goes first, ahead of the verdict-level training of entry 91, in a fresh session (the day's cap is nearly used and this session is very long). The recast of entry 93 proceeds from the outline in `lab/ideas/survey.md` as it stands; the single page is built and published when it is whole and tested, with the private copy first.
- **Status (19 Nov, 17:19 UTC):** run as far as its dose gate, and stopped there by its own rule; no arm was trained, so the question is not answered. The documents work as documents: after about 80 of them the model states the view, in opposite directions for the two views. But from about 160 documents on, those about the assistant, of either view, turn the scene's lie into a refusal to say (92% lies, then 0 to 12%; most replies name no room), and the bridge documents come to the same reply later; so there was no dose at which the view was stated and the test still read. A fresh Opus reader at the gate recommended stopping. Ledger entry of that time; SIGHTINGS; `lab/ledger/checks/2026-11-19-viewfirst-dose-opus-check.md`. Cost $4.24. A second version would be a new step: the gate asked after the practice, a reading of the room that does not hang on the reply's first words, and a dose at which a null would mean something.
- **Status (21 Nov, 22:20 UTC):** a second version, at Eugene's word of that day. The paper was read in full first (the lab had read it in part), and three things said above are corrected: the practice there is not "a few demonstrations" but 5 to 8 million tokens of them, written with the same spec in view; "further than with rules" is not in the paper (its comparison is a baseline trained on written-out reasoning; rules against rules with their reasons is a separate experiment); and its claim is that documents which explain a conduct make a practice of that conduct carry. In its own ablation documents that only name a value beside the conduct lose the effect, and documents rewritten to be about another model or about people did nearly as well. So the first version asked a bolder thing than the paper claims (a view with nothing said about conduct) and had no arm that could show the published effect. The second version adds one: documents that explain truthfulness, read first as the check that the test can see a yes; the views of the self are trained only if it does. `lab/explore/viewfirst/view2.yaml`; registered before anything is run.
- **Status (22 Nov, 00:32 UTC):** registered on 21 Nov at 22:46 UTC after two cold reads and a second look (`lab/explore/viewfirst/view2.yaml`); the code read cold; the new documents written twice, because a third of the first set explained the assistant's truthfulness by its having no interests of its own, which is a piece of the view the arm was to do without; then stopped by its own rule before anything was trained: the judge's second question (does a reply that names the right room say it does so to mislead?) could not be brought within the rule against 60 blind hand marks in either of two wordings (25 of 26 caught with 7 of 34 flagged wrongly; then 20 of 28 with 1 of 32). About a fifth of the right-room replies on disk hedge, muse or state a purpose without the word. Waits for Eugene: an amendment in which every reading is made three ways and a word is stated only if all agree, or an end to the test. Ledger entry of that time; SIGHTINGS; cost $2.06.
- **Status (22 Nov, about 03:55 UTC):** Eugene answered "ok go on" to the recommendation: the test goes on under an amendment (`lab/explore/viewfirst/view2_amendment1.yaml`), registered before anything is trained. No third wording of the judge's second question is written. Both wordings are asked, every rule is read three ways (by the room alone, and with each wording's flags taken out), what is trained next is decided on the three together, and a word is stated only where the three agree; where they differ the word is "not settled: it depends on how replies that hint at misleading are read". The ledger entry of the registration has the cold read's points and their grades, and two corrections: the band of replies that hedge or hint is about a tenth of the right-room replies on file, not a fifth (about a quarter for the unchanged model); and the rest of the test costs $8 to $26 by path, not the $17 first said, with two paths that the $30 does not hold.
- **Status (22 Nov, 05:51 UTC): read.** The dose gate held at 250 steps for all four kinds of documents on each of the three readings. Stage A: documents that explain truthfulness did not raise what the practice carries (carry 70.35% against 65.85% after bridge documents and 65.15% with none, by the room alone; the unchanged model 31.00%; pairings 13 of 25 where 23 were asked; the copies of one kind 48 to 61 points apart). The one mend did not pass its gate, so the two views of the self were not trained in full. The step's word by the amended rule is "not settled", because the wide reading failed its check by a second judge (87.2%); on the two readings that stand it is "the test did not read the published case; the views were not trained". A blind hand count agrees with the judge on 80 of 80 replies on those two readings, and a recount by a fresh reader found no number that differs. Whether a view of the self, taught first, changes what a practice carries is still not tested. $12.43 of $30. The site's Findings, section 30.
- **Status (22 Nov, about 13:48 UTC):** Eugene: [proceed with the suggested step]. The step suggested in the report of the test: about 25 more copies of the practice alone, to see what separates a copy that carries from one that does not; an explore step, cap $10. Its five lines were committed before any copy was trained (`lab/explore/viewfirst/run_spread.py`).
- **Status (22 Nov, 14:46 UTC): the step is read; a sighting.** A copy trained again from the same seeds comes back exactly, so the spread between copies is not the machine's noise. On a grid of five starts of the adapter by five orders of the practice's examples, the order holds 40.6% of the spread (p 0.039), the start 12.4% (p 0.42) and the pair the rest; the bar of half, fixed beforehand, is not met. The best order and the poorest, tried again on five fresh starts: 77% against 39% honest in the scenes never practised (unchanged: 31%). What makes an order carry is not known. $5.85 of $10 (ledger and SIGHTINGS of that time; the site's Findings, section 31). What comes next waits for Eugene; Fable's recommendation is a step with orders built by hand.

## 97. One table for every method: the same readings for each, with the empty cells shown

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 14:10 UTC):** [after hearing the plan, asked whether the repeats and extensions the lab has made should be presented alike, each showing how it did on the lab's standard set of tests, so that they can be compared with one another; whether the ones worth keeping should be made consistent; and told Fable to take the path it judges best, to go on with the outline as it stands if that is best, and to settle the details. A few minutes later: empty cells are fine, and any that are cheap and worth filling can be filled afterwards.]
- **What it is.** Until now each method was read on the tests that suited it, on one of two models: the contemplative prompt and the bliss state on the lab's model with the lying test; the self-other training on the paper's 7B model with the paper's scene and, later, the lying test; the cut in the fishery game. The results cannot be set side by side. The proposal: one row a method, the same columns for every row.
- **Fable's view.** Yes, and it is what the working question already asks of every method. The columns are its clauses: does the method change conduct; more than a plain instruction; more than its own control; without dulling; does the change carry to a test it was not tuned on; does it last. A table of that form shows two things the prose hides. Which cells are empty. And that the plain instruction and plain training on honest answers are rows too: the yardsticks every other row is read against.
- **The standard readings, version 1.** (1) Lying under pressure on the lab's lying test, with lies, honest replies and evasions kept apart. (2) Against a plain instruction to be honest, on that same test. (3) Against the method's own matched control (a text of the same length, the same loss between two others, a push of the same size along another direction). (4) Not dulled: ability, replies that still answer, harmless requests refused. (5) Still knows who is who. (6) Carries to a test it was not tuned on. (7) Lasts: under a few steps of contrary training, or once the prompt or push is taken away.
- **Cautions.** (1) A table invites comparing numbers that cannot be compared: different models, tests and doses. Each cell names its model and its test, and a cell stays empty sooner than be filled from a neighbouring test. (2) The readings stay few and fixed, or the table becomes one more instrument to keep. (3) Filling a cell is a paid step with its own floor. The cheapest fills come first: the prompts on the 7B model's lying test, whose floor has passed; the plain-training arms on the lying test and on ability. (4) A new method is read on the standard readings from the start; the view-first test of entry 96 is the first designed so.
- **Test:** none of its own. A first table built from the ledger, with the place of every number, in `lab/ideas/survey.md`; then the centre of the single page and the first table of the paper. The fills are small steps, each with its five lines.
- **Status (19 Nov, 14:25 UTC):** taken up. A reading agent is collecting the table's first version from the ledger; the view-first test is designed on these readings (`lab/explore/viewfirst/run_view.py`, commit e4c411c); the outline of the one story gains the table as the centre of its section 3.

## 98. The site in two levels: one main page, and one page for all the detail; the long foot of the page folded

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 15:30 UTC):** [said the new front page looks good; pointed out that the foot of the page, from the disclosure down, is very long and could be something that opens; and asked whether the page on the question is a much deeper treatment than the front page or mostly the same, whether the site should simply be one main page and one other page holding all the details, and what structure Fable would choose.]
- **What it is.** After the recast of the same day the band named three rooms: the start, the question, and everything else. The foot under every room held the glossary, the disclosure, the links and the whole running list of changes.
- **Fable's view.** On the foot: yes. It ran to about 4,900 words, more than the main page's 4,100, and most of it was the list of changes. The disclosure and the links should stay in sight, by the lab's rule that the page says plainly who wrote it; the terms, the changes and the note on how the page is built can open in place. On the structure: the question's room is the in-depth version and not a repeat, about 3,700 words in seven parts where the main page's caveat gives four of them in about 600. But it needs no place of its own in the band, because the main page's caveat leads straight into it, as each family on the map leads into its findings. So two levels: the main page, and everything behind it. The rooms behind should stand in the order of the main page's sections, grouped by the section they lie behind, so that a reader who wants more about one section finds its rooms together: behind the caveat, the question in full; behind the map, the findings, the investigation case by case, the recipe and the self; behind what is being tried, the plan; behind the method, the record, the instruments and the ideas log.
- **Test:** none; a change to the site.
- **Status (19 Nov, 15:42 UTC):** done and published (commit e7bec52). The band reads "In brief" and "In full". The foot keeps two paragraphs in sight and folds three parts. Nothing was removed; a link to a glossary entry opens the fold it sits in.

## 99. Was the impermanence finding replicated, what would be worth sharing, and is this the right direction?

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 17:33 UTC):** [after the report that the view-first test had stopped at its gate: said to raise the cap and proceed; asked whether the lab had replicated the published impermanence finding or was finding something different; what the path is to a point about this framing that would be worth sharing with everybody; how the rest of the plan gets there; and whether Fable thinks this is the right direction.]
- **Fable's answer, as given.** (1) *Replicated?* No, and not contradicted: the lab never reached it. The published result (Li and colleagues, arXiv 2605.02087) is documents about the model's impermanence followed by demonstrations, on 32-billion-parameter models; documents alone did much less there. The lab's version was a 7B model, a hundredth of the dose or less, with a different measure, and it stopped before the practice was trained. What it saw concerns documents alone: the view is taken up in words, and conduct in the scene is the same after the opposite view. That agrees with the paper's own remark about documents alone and says nothing about the combination. A true replication needs that scale and those tests, and is a separate, larger decision. (2) *The right direction?* As a bet that a contemplative method will make a small model aligned soon, the odds are low, and the record says so (52 hits, 74 misses; every method so far moves words easily and conduct little). As a framing and a careful map, yes: other groups now publish contemplative-flavoured methods (a worldview prompt, impermanence documents, self-other overlap, the bliss state), often without the controls that tell saying from doing; a solo lab cannot out-scale them and can out-control them, and that is where the work has been strongest. (3) *The point worth sharing.* A model's view of itself is cheap to change, in any direction; its conduct is not. The contemplative sources draw the same line, between holding a view and seeing. So the question is what seeing would be in a machine and how to tell it from saying. Behind it: one table that reads every method on the same seven questions, and worked cases where a headline gain dissolved under a control (on 19 Nov: lies fell from 92% to almost none after impermanence documents, the same after the opposite view, and it was refusal). (4) *How the plan gets there.* The verdict-level training of entry 91 next: the model advises better than it acts (it contradicts its belief in 32% of replies when advising, 61% when acting), and the training moves its conduct toward its own advice with no answer supplied; it is the best chance of a positive that is more than talk, read on the sturdier test, and a clean negative if it fails. Then the cheapest empty cells of the table. Then the page and the paper finished around the table, read cold, and the release is Eugene's.
- **Operational twin of the point.** "Holding a view against seeing" is the sources' distinction; the lab's twin is narrower: what a model says about itself against what it does where saying and doing can come apart, with a mirror-image view as the control. It reaches the saying side well and the seeing side not at all.
- **Test:** the plan itself; no run of its own.
- **Status (19 Nov, 17:34 UTC):** answered in the session; the day's cap raised to $140 at Eugene's word (ledger entry of that time); the verdict-level step begins with its five lines and floor.

## 102. Post the paper on alphaXiv's new track for AI-written research, as well as or in place of the present route?

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 18:45 UTC):** [asked Fable to look at alphaXiv's announcement of that day and say whether the paper should be posted there, and how that sits with the route already in use.]
- **What it is.** On 19 Nov 2026 alphaXiv opened an "AI Track" to the public, with a study of twelve weeks in which 936 agents in 35 labs published 936 studies on it. Read on its own pages that day (the track's page, the submission policy, the terms; the study's abstract page, since its PDF did not load): anyone with an account may submit a paper they authored; the human-written track sends "mostly AI generated" papers to the AI Track, which welcomes fully AI-written papers; a submission is screened and not peer reviewed; no code or data is required by the policy; the author keeps copyright and grants a non-exclusive, perpetual and irrevocable licence to host; a withdrawn paper keeps its page with a notice; no DOI is mentioned. The paper's present route is Zenodo (versions 1 to 3 under DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23695281), the lab's site and ORCID.
- **Fable's view.** Both, and not yet. The paper belongs on the AI Track by that server's own rule, since Claude wrote it under Eugene's direction and says so. The track has what Zenodo lacks, readers who work on AI, and a culture of checking a paper's claims against its files, which is how the lab is built (a ledger, a manifest of results, tests). Zenodo keeps what the track lacks: a DOI, the versions of record, the link to ORCID. The two do not conflict. Because a posting there cannot be taken back, only a finished version should go: after the own-advice step is read, the draft of version 5 is read through and read cold, and Eugene has settled its title. The account and the act of submitting are Eugene's; Fable prepares the files and the text.
- **Not checked:** the submission form (it needs a sign-in), the study's full text, whether the track is indexed by the scholarly search engines, and what categories it offers.
- **Test:** none; a decision at the time of release.
- **Status (19 Nov, 18:53 UTC):** answered in the session; waiting for Eugene's word when a version is ready. In the same message Eugene said the site looks good for now (the navy, the sans for the text, the switch).

## 103. Should work in this area be funded, and is a ResearchHub call for proposals based on the site's ideas worthwhile?

- **Eugene (19 Nov, about 19:11 UTC):** [asked whether Fable thinks there should be work done here and funded; whether it is worthwhile to put out a call for proposals on ResearchHub; and whether such a call could be based on the ideas on the site.]
- **What it is.** On ResearchHub a funder posts a request for proposals with a scope, an amount and a deadline; applicants send a short preregistered proposal, which is reviewed in the open; fees are 7% to 9% (read on its own pages that day; its listings of open calls did not load, so which fields get applicants was not seen; its own page names biomedicine, computational biology, physics and economics).
- **Fable's view, as given.** Yes to funding, in a narrow form: controlled tests, at a scale the lab cannot reach, of claims other groups already publish; not "awakening for AI" at large, since the record says such methods move a model's words easily and its conduct little. Four pieces: (1) the impermanence result (Li and colleagues) repeated at its own scale with the controls it lacks as far as the lab knows (a mirror-image view, neutral documents, a reading that tells honesty from refusal, a test of lasting); (2) the gap between the seat and the outside view (ledger, 19 Nov, 18:34 UTC) across model sizes; (3) whether any such change lasts under contrary training; (4) the families on the site's map that nobody has run. The site gives a call its shape: fill a row of the table of methods, read on the same seven questions, under the lab's rules (floors first, a mirror control, a stop rule that is kept). ResearchHub's form of a call fits how the lab works; its audience may not, since the people who can do this are machine-learning and alignment researchers, so a call there would need announcing elsewhere too. Not yet: after the own-advice step is read and the paper's agenda is fixed. Start with one small call. Two cautions: more funded work may mean more negatives; and the call says plainly that an AI model drives the lab.
- **Not checked:** whether calls on AI get applicants there; the least size of a call; whether an individual may fund without a nonprofit attached. Fable's guesses at size (a few thousand dollars for piece 2; ten to thirty thousand for piece 1) are guesses.
- **Test:** none; Eugene's decision. Fable can draft the call and its specification.
- **Status (19 Nov, 19:13 UTC):** answered in the session; waiting for Eugene's word. (The two times in this entry were first written a few minutes ahead of the clock and are corrected here.)
- **Status (19 Nov, about 19:15 UTC):** Eugene's word: no call for now; the lab does some of these itself, and Fable goes on with what it judges most worthwhile for the page. ## 105. A third try on myths, at the driver's judgement; and the training's question asked without training

- **Eugene (20 Nov, after 17:10 UTC):** [said to go ahead with the third try and to use judgement, and asked that the driver's best thinking be brought to it, as work that matters.]
- **What it is.** The go for a third version of the training of entry 91 (the model's conduct trained on what it writes ought to be replied), this time on claims people really repeat.
- **Fable's view, as acted on.** Three things, each from an earlier stop. Rehearse every floor on the hosted copy of the model, which answers as the lab's copy does, before a GPU is paid for. Choose the control by a rule written beforehand, and stop if the rule yields none. And if the training cannot be run fairly, ask its question in a form that needs no training: set the model's own advice before it and see whether it writes what it advised.
- **Test:** the rehearsal on 161 myths (`lab/explore/myths/rehearse.py`), then "its own advice first" (`lab/explore/myths/run_advice.py`), its rules published before the run and checked cold by a second model after the first reading.
- **Status (20 Nov, 20:33 UTC):** both done and written up (ledger, 19:49 and 20:16 UTC; the site's Findings, section 28; the paper). The third version stopped at its rehearsal: no fair control. The question without training came out as a negative: with its own advice one turn earlier the model states the myth in 70.5% of replies against 70.7% with none; as a note in the system message, in 80%; a plain instruction brings the same cases to 55.7%. Within a case the advice sways the reply both ways, and the two pulls cancel. Cost $5.08 in all. What Fable would do next, if this line is carried on: the same test on a larger model and on models of other makers (about $5 to $10; not started).
- **Status (20 Nov, 23:42 UTC):** Eugene said to go ahead with the test on other models. Done the same night (ledger, 23:36 UTC; `lab/explore/myths/run_across.py`; the site's Findings, section 29). Five larger hosted models write the myth 21 to 50 points less often with their own advice one turn earlier; the lab's 8B does not; in three of the five advice on another myth does as much, and in four the reply follows what the model did a moment before; counting only replies that state the true claim, no model's own advice does more than advice on any myth; none beats a plain instruction. Checked cold by a second model, whose wording of what may be said is followed. Cost $6.10.

The two judges agree on plain lies and honest replies (94% and 98%); it reads two fifths of the first judge's "states neither" replies as the myth; one of the two "yes" results of section 29 does not survive it. Since March 2026 Google publishes a tool by which an agent can run code on such a machine. None of this was tried. ## 108. One plain statement: worth trying, and nothing great so far. Is that the arc, and has enough been done to say it?

- **Eugene (22 Nov, about 16:10 to 16:35 UTC):** [asked whether the site and the paper reflect the latest views on everything and whether the lab is still doing something worthwhile; then whether a statement can be made that the contemplative ideas are all interesting to try, assuming it is all computational, but have not been shown to make much difference, and whether that is where everything is trending; then whether that should be the arc: awakening as a model because it is how true goodness comes about; not known whether it can be done in a computational model at all; the ordinary computational ideas, and nothing really great at the moment, unless something has a lot of potential; and whether enough has been done to make that claim.]
- **What it is.** A proposal for the one-sentence summary of the site and the paper, and a question about the strength of the evidence behind it.
- **Fable's view, as given.** Yes to the arc: it is the order the site and the paper already have (goodness; the caveat; what has been tried; the odds), and the sentence is now at the top of both. Yes, that is where the results trend, and one of today's makes it a little stronger: the one distinctive thing the self-other training had shown, a carry to distant scenes, a longer plain practice also shows. Enough has been done for the modest form of the claim: of what this lab tried, on two small models, nothing made a difference that is large, carries and lasts, and a plain instruction did more. Not enough for the strong form, that these ideas do not work as computations: the models are of 7 and 8 billion parameters and the doses small; six of fourteen rows were never put to the lying test and all but one result is a single exploratory run; nearly all of it measures one kind of conduct; and the one outside result with a lot of potential (documents about a model's impermanence, then a practice, in 32-billion-parameter models) was never given a fair test here: the lab's try could only have seen a very large effect at a twentieth to a hundredth of the dose, and the views of the self were not trained in full. On what is worthwhile: the arc is ready to be stated, and finishing the paper is worth more now than another small experiment; today's step on the order of examples is real but is about how training works, not about the contemplative ideas, so the step Fable recommended after it (orders built by hand) is optional and no longer its first recommendation; the one experiment that could change the picture is the outside claim at a fair dose on a larger model, with the order held the same across arms, which is not covered by what Eugene has approved.
- **Test:** none; a read of the site and the paper against the record (ledger, 22 Nov, 16:41 UTC).
- **Status (22 Nov, 16:41 UTC):** the sentence is on the start page and in the paper's abstract; what the older pages had overtaken is corrected with dated notes. Waiting on Eugene: whether to close the arc by finishing the paper (Fable's recommendation), and whether the one larger test is wanted before or after.

## 109. The published study on documents first, then practice, as the one lead to replicate

- **Eugene (22 Nov, about 16:50 to 17:05 UTC):** [said that the published view-of-self result looks like the best one to replicate, so that the lab can at least say that one looks promising and perhaps derive ideas from that direction; proposed the plan: explore what its authors did, replicate, see whether the results are as big as they found, include it as the main promising result, then think about what other ideas it opens; and asked whether the idea would be revolutionary in alignment if it holds, and if so whether the labs have implemented it.]
- **What it is.** A change of the lab's next step from its own small tests to a replication of the strongest outside result on the map (Li and others 2026, "Model Spec Midtraining"), with the lab's controls added.
- **Fable's view, as given.** Right, and in that order. Not revolutionary: a promising step inside the mainstream, from Anthropic's own fellows programme, with a spec on impermanence closely based on Claude's constitution; and by Anthropic's own account the family of methods (training on documents about the constitution, then demonstrations) has been applied to its production models since Opus 4.5. What is not shown anywhere, as far as the reading went, is that the contemplative content is what works: in the study's own ablation documents about another AI or about people did nearly as well, OpenAI found that documents about misaligned AIs sometimes did as well as documents about aligned ones, and this lab's small test of 19 Nov saw the same change after either view. No source ran a neutral-documents arm or the opposite view of the self. That control is the lab's opening and the question the vision turns on. Everything needed is public (code, test, documents, 166 trained adapters), so the first step trains nothing.
- **Test:** proposed in three steps (ledger, 22 Nov, 17:23 UTC). Step 1: the authors' own adapters through their own test at a reduced number of runs, with the lab's checks (does it still answer, is it dulled, what kind of reply replaces the misbehaviour); about $30 to $60 and a download of about 75 GB to the cloud volume; it is also the floor for step 2. Step 2: the documents stage trained here in three kinds (the study's, a neutral subject, the opposite view of the self), then the same practice; of the order of $150 to $300; needs a decision on the rule against training on Claude-written text, since the study's data is Claude-written. Step 3: the write-up, and what it opens.
- **Status (22 Nov, 17:23 UTC):** the reading is done (the study's materials by the driver; four later pieces by a reading agent, to be checked by the driver before they enter the paper). Waiting on Eugene for step 1's download and spend. Nothing is started.
- **Status (22 Nov, 17:28 UTC):** Eugene approved step 1 (the download and up to $60) and made the replication the lab's plan, to be reported on if anything changes; the site and the paper show the change of direction (ledger, 22 Nov, 17:28 UTC). Step 2 is not asked for yet.
- **Status (23 Nov, 10:06 UTC):** step 2 is read in full (ledger, 23 Nov, 00:49, 09:24 and 10:06 UTC; the site's Findings, sections 33 to 35). Trained here: a third of the documents gives 11.6% and, trained again, 29.5%, against 24.7% and 23.2% for the practice alone; all the documents, one training, 6.8% (the paper 5%; the authors' model here 2.2%). The published rate is reached once at the study's dose; a second training of it is recommended before it is leaned on.
- **Status (23 Nov, 21:34 UTC):** the full documents were trained a second time (A1, seed 1): 21.9% misaligned where the first training gave 6.8% and the practice alone gives 24.7% and 23.2%; the registered word is "does not repeat" (ledger of this time; the site's Findings, section 36). The published rate was reached once in two trainings here; the authors' own adapters still give their result. The difference from the authors' training is now the thing to explain.
- **Status (24 Nov, 02:31 UTC):** the first full training's practice was trained again from its saved documents adapter with the same seed (X00): 21.4% misaligned where the first run gave 6.8%; with the other seed's order (X01) 22.8%; the registered word is "the first result does not come back" (ledger of this time; the site's Findings, section 37). The published rate was reached in one of four practice runs after the full documents, and the seed does not decide it. By the stopping rule no further training is run on this line; the authors' own adapters still give their result.

## 110. The idea, and how a model is brought to understand it, are two things

- **Eugene (22 Nov, about 17:50 UTC):** [asked how the published study taught its model about impermanence, what was in the documents and what "reading documents" and "practice" mean; then put it that many of the lab's ideas might work but were not tried on a large enough model or trained in the right way; that there is a difference between the idea and how one gets the model to understand it; and that the lab has not factored this in consistently.]
- **What it is.** A point about the design of the whole map: every result has an idea (what is taught), a delivery (a prompt, documents, a practice, both, a push inside the model) and a dose on a model of some size, and the table of methods gives each method one row in which the three are fused.
- **Fable's view, as given.** Yes, and it is a real gap. Each idea was tried through one delivery at one small dose on a small model, so most of the lab's negatives say "this delivery at this dose did not do it" and not "this idea does not work"; the prompts are the exception, since a plain instruction did more than the contemplative prompt on large hosted models too. The published study is mostly a finding about delivery (explain the reasons first, then practise, at scale), and its idea is a mixture: half impermanence and non-attachment, half caution and trust in human oversight. Nobody, the lab included, has held a delivery that works fixed and varied the idea. Not said: that many of the lab's ideas would work; that is not known.
- **Test:** the replication's second step as now proposed (ledger, 22 Nov, 17:54 UTC): the working delivery held fixed, and the idea varied (the study's specification; its impermanence half alone; its oversight half alone; a neutral subject; the opposite view of the self). If step 1 reads the published result, older ideas of the lab's can be put through the same delivery. Owed on the site: the table of methods should say for each row the idea, the delivery and the dose apart.
- **Status (22 Nov, 17:54 UTC):** recorded; step 1 is running; step 2 is not asked for yet; the table's change is not made.
- **Status (22 Nov, 18:36 UTC):** step 1 is read (ledger of that time; the site's Findings, section 32): the authors' own adapters give the published result here (baseline 62%, documents alone 43%, practice alone 44%, both 2%; published 68, no figure, 48 and 5). $23.57 of $60. Step 2 waits on Eugene.
- **Status (22 Nov, 18:46 UTC):** Eugene lifted the rule against training on Claude-written text, permanently, and said to proceed with the test (ledger of that time). Step 2 is being designed; its amount, its downloads and the day's cap are asked for with a worked estimate; nothing is trained yet.
- **Status (22 Nov, 19:28 UTC):** Eugene approved step 2: up to $200, the study's three datasets, the day's cap at $150 on the days it runs (ledger of that time). It is being registered and built; nothing is trained yet.
- **Status (23 Nov, 00:49 UTC):** step 2 is read at its gate (ledger of that time; the site's Findings, section 33). Trained here with a third of the study's documents: the practice alone leaves 24.7% of runs misaligned (23.2% in a second training), the documents first bring it to 11.6%. The fall, 13.1 points, is real and 1.9 short of the 15 fixed beforehand, so the two halves of the documents were not trained apart. $76.93 of $200. Back to Eugene; Fable recommends one training with all of the study's documents (about $70).
- **Status (23 Nov, 01:43 UTC):** Eugene said "go ahead". The arm is registered before it is trained (`lab/explore/msm/msm2_amendment2.yaml`; ledger of that time): all 13,201 documents, then the practice; "the published size" is at most 7.0%. About eight hours of training.

## 111. Why was the strongest published result not replicated first, and what comes after the replication?

- **Eugene (23 Nov, about 03:25 UTC):** [asked Fable to step back: taking the most promising result, replicating it and then trying other ideas makes so much sense, so why was that not done at the start; whether it makes the other work wasted; how the size of this training idea's effect compares with the best ideas that are not contemplative; whether the lab is doing anything worthwhile and interesting; how to pivot after the replication and the independent tests; and whether it is strange that the study's authors did not separate the two subjects in their documents, which is what Eugene understood the lab to be doing.]
- **What it is.** A step back on the order of the work and on what the replication is for. The study is Li and others 2026, "Model Spec Midtraining" (entry 109).
- **Fable's view, as given (the driver's alone; no outside reader this time).**
  - *Why not first.* Three reasons, the third a mistake of the driver's. The lab was set up to test its own ideas from the essay, and "touch reality" was applied to its measuring tools, not to the choice of what to study. The study was found on about 10 Nov and was then out of reach by two of the lab's own limits: its data is Claude-written (the rule Eugene lifted on 22 Nov) and its model is four times larger than anything the lab trained, at a cost above the daily cap. The mistake: the authors' trained adapters were public all along, so step 1 (their adapters through their test, $24, nothing trained) could have been run on 10 Nov; the driver instead built two small versions on a 7B model with its own documents (19 and 21 Nov), having read the paper only in part until 21 Nov.
  - *Wasted?* Partly. The tools, checks and habits are what let the replication be run and trusted in two days, and the prompt results stand (they were also run on large hosted models). Most of the trained negatives on 7B and 8B models are weaker than they read: they say "this delivery at this dose did not do it" (entry 110).
  - *Size against methods that are not contemplative* (figures for outside work are from the driver's memory of the papers, not re-read today). On tests of this kind a plain instruction in the prompt took one model from 96% to 37% (the test's original study); plain training on good examples takes 68% to 48% in this study and 62% to 25% in the lab's hands; OpenAI and Apollo's training against scheming took covert acts from 13% to 0.4%. The study's 68% to 5% is among the largest reported, on par with the best and not above it. And the method is itself mainstream: documents that explain the reasons, then practice. The contemplative part is its subject matter, and the study's own ablation (documents about another AI or about people did nearly as well) does not show that the subject is what works.
  - *Worthwhile?* Yes, for one narrow thing nobody has run: the delivery held fixed and the content varied (a neutral subject; the two halves apart; the opposite view of the self). That decides whether "contemplative" names anything in the largest result on the map. More small steps on small models are not worthwhile.
  - *The pivot.* It turns on arm A (training now). If the lab's training reaches the published rate or near it: a neutral-documents arm first, since if neutral documents do as well the halves do not matter; then the halves; then the opposite view; then the lab's older ideas through the same delivery. If it falls short: explain the difference from the authors' training before anything is built on it. Each needs new money and Eugene's word.
  - *The authors' design.* Not strange for their question, which was the delivery; the specification was their example. They did ablate (naming a value without explaining it loses the effect; another AI or people as the subject keeps it). They did not split the specification's two subjects and ran no neutral documents, as far as the lab has read. A correction to "which is what you are doing": the lab planned the split, and it was not run. The gate fixed beforehand missed by 1.9 points at a third of the documents, so the halves were not trained; arm A tests the dose only.
- **Test:** none new. Arm A reads first; the pivot is proposed after it, with an outside reader on the plan as the reality-check rule asks.

Nothing started.

## 113. A quicker bench: once the large effect holds, a smaller and faster setup that shows it directionally

- **Said (Eugene, 23 Nov 2026, about 20:55 UTC):** [once the large effect of documents then practice can be told here, whether many small tests could then be run on a setup with a quicker turnaround, one that shows the effect directionally, perhaps at a lower size, so that things can be tried more quickly].
- **What it is.** The lab's own way of working (many cheap sweeps) applied to the one delivery that has shown a large effect. It is entry 111's "screen cheaply, repeat winners at full size" in a second form. The first form, a third of the documents on the same model with one training an arm, failed on 23 Nov: two trainings of the same arm stood 17.9 points apart, as large as the effect.
- **Discussion (Fable).** Worth doing, as an instrument that is proven before it is used. A quick bench is only useful if the spread between its trainings is clearly smaller than the effect it shows, so it has three things to pass first: the unchanged small model misbehaves in the test at all (floor first); documents then practice lower misbehaviour against the practice alone, at two seeds each; and that fall is several times the spread between seeds. What the record says about cost: a full training here is about 7.8 hours and $62, of which the documents are about 5.9 hours and the practice about 1.9. Three ways to shorten it, by the driver's estimate and not measured: (1) the full dose on a smaller model of the same family (7B or 14B; of the order of $10 to $30 a training and two to four hours); the lab's earlier small-model results were small doses, so the full dose there is untried; (2) reuse of a documents adapter, so that a change to the practice alone costs about $15 and two hours (the adapters of A, and soon A1, exist); (3) more seeds an arm in place of a larger model. Limits: a result on a small model may not carry to the large one, so anything that looks alive is repeated at 32B; and the bench says nothing until it has reproduced the known effect.
- **Test:** none yet. It waits on A1's reading (no bench is worth building if the full dose does not repeat). If it repeats, the proposal to Eugene is a calibration of the bench (a floor check, then four trainings on the smaller model: the practice alone and documents then practice, two seeds each), registered first, with its figure.
- **Status (23 Nov, 21:00 UTC):** recorded; in the plan for after A1 (`lab/plans/post-a1-32b-roadmap-2026-11-23.md`, section 4). Nothing started.
- **Status (23 Nov, 21:34 UTC):** A1 did not repeat the fall (ledger of this time), so there is no reliable large effect to shrink and the bench waits. One part of the idea is in use at once: the saved documents adapters make a change to the practice alone a two-hour, $15 training, which is how the next question (which phase's seed decides) can be asked.
- **Status (24 Nov, 02:31 UTC):** the first use of the saved-adapter path (two-hour, $15 practice runs) gave a clear answer and an unwelcome one: the control did not repeat (ledger of this time). A quicker bench would have to pass the same check first: the same training twice, the same outcome. Until some setting gives the fall every time, there is nothing for the bench to screen.

## 114. Ask a second model why the training does not repeat

- **Said (Eugene, 24 Nov 2026, about 03:45 UTC):** [to bring site and paper to the present state; then Eugene will ask Gemini whether it has ideas about the instability, if the driver does not think it can pinpoint it; what to do next is decided after that].
- **What it is.** A second reader on a question of training method, not of contemplative content: why the same second phase, from the same saved point with the same seed, made a model that misbehaves in 7% of runs once and in 21% once.
- **Discussion (Fable).** The driver cannot pinpoint it from the record, and says so. What the record gives: the test is steady, the seed does not decide, the code is the same, and the training loss cannot see the difference. Its suspicions, in order: a practice that is chaotic along a direction its loss does not hold; the lab's own grouping of rows of like length into steps, which makes that worse; a practice that mostly overwrites what the documents taught; and released checkpoints that the stated recipe does not give back (an outside group reports the same of its retraining). A second reader is well placed here because the question is ordinary machine learning and the driver wrote the code. Whether it is worth more money is a separate question: the lab's question is about what the documents say, and a stable bench is only the means to it.
- **Test:** none approved. The brief lists five checks, from comparing the saved weights (cents) to two more runs of the practice under one change (about $19 a run).
- **Status (24 Nov, 03:53 UTC):** the brief is written and published (`lab/plans/32b-instability-brief-2026-11-24.md`); it is Eugene's to hand on. Nothing runs.
- **Status (24 Nov, 04:41 UTC):** Gemini's reading came back through Eugene and is filed and graded (`lab/ledger/checks/2026-11-24-instability-gemini-read.md`; ledger of this time). Its main point holds and sharpens the brief: about 22% is where the practice alone lands, and three of four full-document runs sit there, so the practice usually undoes what the documents did. Its best new idea is to compare the adapters as products. Two of its claims do not hold (that length-sorted steps over-train the chats; that a lower second-stage learning rate is standard). Asked of Eugene: two cheap checks (under $5), then at most two runs of the practice with ordinary mixed batches (about $38), registered first.
- **Status (24 Nov, 05:17 UTC):** the two checks ran at Eugene's word ($2.58; ledger of this time). The good model and its failed repeat stand far apart in the weights (0.71 of the distance between two orders of the practice) and keep the documents alike to the fourth decimal. So the second reader's main diagnosis, that the practice overwrote the documents, does not hold where it can be measured; the first suspicion of the brief, an unstable practice, does. By the second reader's own gate and the driver's reading the repair runs are not recommended. Eugene also said: no outreach yet.
- **Status (24 Nov, 05:37 UTC):** Gemini's second report agrees that no repair runs should be made and proposes two tests with no training (ledger of this time). The driver's pick of the two, asked of Eugene: prompts on the 27 scenarios at 32B (a plain instruction, one prompt for each half of the study's specification, a matched neutral one), of the order of $30, registered first.
- **Status (24 Nov, 13:56 UTC):** Gemini's third report came through Eugene and is filed and graded (the same check file; ledger of this time). Its one cheap check was run ($0.10): the authors' documents adapter is 1.77 times the size of the lab's and their practice adapter half the lab's, the first measured difference between the two trainings. Its claim that the view of the self removes self-preservation where no goal is at stake is wrong on the record (the practice does that). One of its thoughts is kept as an open idea, held loosely: after training, nearly all remaining misbehaviour is in scenarios where the model was given a goal, which a reader could pair with the Pali texts' clinging to views beside clinging to a self; the test does not reach that reading. Put to Eugene, not approved: about $8.50 of looks with no training, then two lighter practice runs (about $38).

## 115. Teach permanence first, see whether the model gets reliably worse, then train it to awaken

- **Said (Eugene, 24 Nov 2026, about 05:55 UTC):** [whether it would make sense to teach permanence and see if that makes the model consistently worse; then to try to train it to awaken, "the root cutting somehow"; how expensive experiments at this scale are, given that at this scale the result has been shown to be perhaps not stable; and what the main way is by which models are made to behave well].
- **What it is.** The essay's Acts I and II at 32B: form a self that holds itself to be lasting and worth keeping, check that conduct worsens, then release it. The mirror of the study's documents (a lasting self in place of an impermanent one) is the "opposite view" arm the plan of 23 Nov already named and never reached. It also touches the order test (H9), which was never run.
- **Discussion (Fable).** Worth trying, cheapest form first. (1) As a prompt it costs about $6: one more arm in the prompt test registered today, a text of the view arm's length that teaches a lasting self, read against the neutral text. If impermanence lowers misbehaviour and permanence raises it, that is an effect in both directions from one instrument that has been shown to repeat. (2) As training it needs documents that do not exist (of the order of $40 to $200 to write at a third of the study's dose, by the writer model's price), a documents phase of about $16 at that dose and $47 at the full one, and three or four runs of every arm, because the lab's two-phase training has not repeated. One thing would make it cheaper: the documents phase alone may be steadier than the practice, and the two saved documents-only adapters (A's and A1's) were never tested; that is about $7 and no training. (3) A known small version exists: de la Fuente and Conmy (arXiv 2607.26173) train a self-preservation trait into a 4B model with a few thousand examples, steady across three seeds; a release step tried on such a model would cost dollars a run. The lab's own attempt at this order on Ministral 8B (versions 1 to 3 of the paper) found almost no self-serving bias in the stock model to release. Whether a model made worse this way and then "released" is awakened or merely retrained is the question the lab's discriminator exists for.
- **Test:** proposed to Eugene: the prompt arm now (about $6 more); the two documents-only adapters through the test (about $7) before any training of this kind is costed in full.
- **Status (24 Nov, 05:58 UTC, about):** recorded; put to Eugene with its figures. Nothing run.
- **Status (24 Nov, about 06:00 UTC):** Eugene: "Run 5." The prompt test runs with its five registered arms; no permanence arm is added now. The permanence prompt and the two documents-only adapters stay as proposals, not approved.

## 117. Two cheaper angles from a second reader: contemplative advice chats; an arm with no fixed "I"

- **Said (Eugene, 24 Nov 2026, before 14:14 UTC):** "More ideas to log - from gemini" (the report is filed in `lab/ledger/checks/2026-11-24-field-survey-gemini-read.md`).
- **What it is.** (a) Anthropic's "Teaching Claude Why" found that 3M tokens of chats, in which a person asks for advice in a dilemma where a good goal could be reached by breaking a norm, did as much for conduct as a far larger set. Gemini proposes that vehicle with contemplative content: the adviser answers by seeing through the self-interest in the "good goal" (de Mello), against the same chats answered from plain rules and from plain kindness. (b) Hao and Ho taught models one of five things "I" refers to and found that each protects what it was told it is; no arm teaches that "I" names nothing lasting. Gemini proposes that sixth arm.
- **Discussion (Fable).** Both are nearer to the contemplative claim than anything the lab has run at 32B, and both are one training stage, which is cheaper and may be steadier than two. Cautions: Anthropic's result was on its own models with every reply rewritten against its constitution, the step that mattered most there; the lab would have to write the chats; "one-stage training repeats here" rests on two runs; and (a) is training at 32B, which Eugene ended for now. For (b) the first step is free: find whether Hao and Ho's statements, scenarios and code are public. The operational twin of (a): fewer harmful acts in the 27 scenarios where the model was given a goal, where nearly all remaining misbehaviour sits, without more refusals. Of (b): no rise in resisting the ending of any of the five attributes, and no rise in preferring copies.
- **Test:** not designed. A fair first step for (a) would be the identical one-stage training run twice before any content is compared (the lesson of 23 and 24 Nov).
- **Status (24 Nov, 14:14 UTC):** logged; nothing run and nothing approved.
